#### Current Configuration:

**In `tracing.py`:**
```python
@contextmanager
def pipeline_span(
    name: str,
    span_type: str = "CHAIN",
    protocol_id: str = "",
):
    """Create a separate MLflow trace for a pipeline node.
    
    Each call creates its own top-level trace tagged with protocol_id,
    so individual node traces appear in MLflow as they complete rather
    than waiting for the entire pipeline to finish.
    """
    try:
        import mlflow
        if os.getenv("MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI"):
            with mlflow.start_span(name=name, span_type=span_type) as span:
                if protocol_id:
                    try:
                        mlflow.update_current_trace(
                            tags={"protocol_id": protocol_id},
                        )
                    except Exception:
                        logger.debug("MLflow tag update failed (non-fatal)")
                yield span
                return
    except ImportError:
        logger.debug("mlflow not installed, skipping tracing")
    except Exception:
        logger.debug("MLflow span creation failed, falling back to no-op")
    
    # Fallback: no-op span
    yield _NoOpSpan()
```

**Key Design:**
- Creates separate top-level traces per node (not child spans)
- Each trace is tagged with `protocol_id` for session grouping in MLflow UI
- Falls back to no-op span if MLflow unavailable
- Uses context manager pattern for proper cleanup

**In `main.py` (API Service):**
```python

    import mlflow
    tracking_uri = os.getenv("MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI")

        mlflow.set_tracking_uri(tracking_uri)
        mlflow.set_experiment("protocol-processing")
        # Enable LangChain autolog for extraction/grounding agent traces

### 2. LangGraph Graph Definition and Invocation

#### Main Files:
- **`/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/graph.py`** - Graph definition

- **`/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/trigger.py`** - Graph invocation

#### Graph Structure:

**7-Node Pipeline:**
```
START -> ingest -> extract -> parse -> ground -> persist -> structure -> ordinal_resolve -> END
```

**In `state.py` - PipelineState TypedDict:**
```python
class PipelineState(TypedDict):
    # Input (always present)
    protocol_id: str
    file_uri: str
    title: str
    
    # Created by parse node
    batch_id: str | None
    
    # Processing artifacts (populated on-demand)
    pdf_bytes: bytes | None
    extraction_json: str | None
    entities_json: str | None
    grounded_entities_json: str | None
    
    # Re-extraction context (optional)
    archived_reviewed_criteria: list[dict] | None
    
    # Ordinal resolution
    ordinal_proposals_json: str | None
    
    # Output
    status: Literal["processing", "completed", "failed"]
    error: str | None
    errors: list[str]  # Accumulate partial failures
```

**In `graph.py` - Graph Creation:**
```python
def create_graph(checkpointer: Any = None) -> Any:
    """Create and compile the 7-node protocol processing workflow graph."""
    workflow = StateGraph(PipelineState)
    
    # Add all 7 nodes
    workflow.add_node("ingest", ingest_node)
    workflow.add_node("extract", extract_node)
    workflow.add_node("parse", parse_node)
    workflow.add_node("ground", ground_node)
    workflow.add_node("persist", persist_node)
    workflow.add_node("structure", structure_node)
    workflow.add_node("ordinal_resolve", ordinal_resolve_node)
    
    # Entry point
    workflow.add_edge(START, "ingest")
    
    # Conditional error routing after ingest, extract, parse
    for source, target in [
        ("ingest", "extract"),
        ("extract", "parse"),
        ("parse", "ground"),
    ]:
        workflow.add_conditional_edges(
            source,
            should_continue,
            {"continue": target, "error": END},
        )
    
    # Ground always proceeds to persist (error accumulation)
    workflow.add_edge("ground", "persist")
    workflow.add_edge("persist", "structure")
    workflow.add_edge("structure", "ordinal_resolve")
    workflow.add_edge("ordinal_resolve", END)
    
    return workflow.compile(checkpointer=checkpointer)
```

**Checkpointing Mechanism:**
- Uses AsyncPostgresSaver singleton
- Lazily initialized on first graph request via `_get_checkpointer_async()`
- Context manager kept open for app lifetime
- Creates checkpoint tables automatically via `.setup()`

### 3. Async Patterns with LangGraph

#### Files with Async Invocation:

**`/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/trigger.py`:**

```python
async def _run_pipeline(
    initial_state: dict[str, Any],
    config: dict[str, Any],
    payload: dict[str, Any],
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Run the pipeline graph with MLflow tracing inside the async context.
    
    MLflow ContextVars must be created in the same async context as the
    LangGraph invocation. asyncio.run() creates an isolated context, so
    we initialize MLflow tracing HERE (inside asyncio.run) rather than
    in the sync caller.
    """
    from protocol_processor.graph import get_graph
    
    graph = await get_graph()
    
    if _ensure_mlflow():
        import mlflow
        mlflow.langchain.autolog(run_tracer_inline=True)
        logging.getLogger("mlflow.utils.autologging_utils").setLevel(logging.ERROR)
    
    return await graph.ainvoke(initial_state, config)


def handle_protocol_uploaded(payload: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    """Handle a ProtocolUploaded event by running the full pipeline.
    
    The handler is synchronous (called by OutboxProcessor.poll_and_process) and
    bridges to the async graph via asyncio.run(). This works without event loop
    conflicts because the outbox processor runs handlers in a thread executor
    via run_in_executor, so there is no existing event loop in the current thread.
    """
    protocol_id = payload.get("protocol_id", "unknown")
    
    try:
        thread_id = f"{protocol_id}:{uuid4()}"
        
        initial_state: dict[str, Any] = {
            "protocol_id": payload["protocol_id"],
            "file_uri": payload["file_uri"],
            "title": payload["title"],
            "batch_id": None,
            "pdf_bytes": None,
            "extraction_json": None,
            "entities_json": None,
            "grounded_entities_json": None,
            "archived_reviewed_criteria": payload.get("archived_reviewed_criteria"),
            "status": "processing",
            "error": None,
            "errors": [],
        }
        
        config = {"configurable": {"thread_id": thread_id}}
        
        asyncio.run(_run_pipeline(initial_state, config, payload))
        
    except Exception as e:
        logger.exception("Protocol pipeline failed for protocol %s", protocol_id)
        raise
```

**Key Async Pattern:**
1. Outbox processor runs handlers in a thread executor (ThreadPoolExecutor)
2. Handler calls `asyncio.run()` to create isolated event loop (no conflicts)
3. `_run_pipeline()` is async, initializes MLflow tracing inside the context
4. Calls `await graph.ainvoke(initial_state, config)`
5. Thread ID includes UUID4 to prevent checkpoint collision on re-extraction

**Checkpoint Recovery:**
```python
async def retry_from_checkpoint(protocol_id: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Resume pipeline from last checkpoint for a failed protocol.
    
    Passes None as the input state to graph.ainvoke, which tells LangGraph
    to resume from the last saved checkpoint for the given thread_id.
    """
    with Session(engine) as session:
        protocol = session.get(Protocol, protocol_id)
        if not protocol:
            raise ValueError(f"Protocol {protocol_id} not found")
        thread_id = (protocol.metadata_ or {}).get("pipeline_thread_id", protocol_id)
    
    from protocol_processor.graph import get_graph
    
    graph = await get_graph()
    config = {"configurable": {"thread_id": thread_id}}
    result = await graph.ainvoke(None, config)
    return result
```

async def extract_node(state: PipelineState) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Extract structured criteria from PDF using Gemini File API."""
    if state.get("error"):
        return {}
    
    protocol_id = state.get("protocol_id", "")
    with pipeline_span(
        "extract_node", span_type="LLM", protocol_id=protocol_id
    ) as span:
        span.set_inputs({
            "protocol_id": state.get("protocol_id", ""),
            "title": state.get("title", ""),
            "pdf_bytes_len": len(state.get("pdf_bytes") or b""),
        })
        
        try:
            extraction_json = await extract_criteria_structured(
                pdf_bytes=state["pdf_bytes"],
                protocol_id=state["protocol_id"],
                title=state["title"],
            )
            
            span.set_outputs({"extraction_json_len": len(extraction_json) if extraction_json else 0})
            return {"extraction_json": extraction_json, "pdf_bytes": None}
        
        except Exception as e:
            logger.exception("Extraction failed for protocol %s: %s", state.get("protocol_id", "unknown"), e)
            span.set_outputs({"error": str(e)})
            return {"error": f"Extraction failed: {e}"}

**Ground Node (async with parallel processing):**
```python
async def _ground_entity_parallel(
    entity: dict[str, Any],
    router: TerminologyRouter,
    criterion_text: str,
    entity_num: int,
    total: int,
    semaphore: asyncio.Semaphore,
) -> tuple[EntityGroundingResult | None, str | None, float, int]:
    """Ground a single entity with concurrency control and timing.
    
    Acquires the semaphore before grounding to cap concurrent API calls.
    """
    async with semaphore:
        entity_text = entity.get("text", "")[:50]
        entity_type = entity.get("entity_type", "")
        start = time.monotonic()
        
        try:
            # Dual grounding: run TerminologyRouter + OMOP mapper in parallel per entity
            full_entity_text = entity.get("text", "")
            tu_task = _ground_entity_with_retry(entity, router, criterion_text)
            omop_task = lookup_omop_concept(full_entity_text, entity_type)
            (result, retry_attempts), omop_result = await asyncio.gather(
                tu_task, omop_task
            )
            
            # Reconcile dual grounding results
            result = _reconcile_dual_grounding(result, omop_result)
            
            # Generate field mappings (same semaphore slot)
            field_mappings = await generate_field_mappings(result, criterion_text)
            result.field_mappings = field_mappings if field_mappings else None
            
            return (result, None, elapsed_ms, retry_attempts)
        except Exception as e:
            elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
            elapsed_ms = elapsed * 1000
            return (None, str(e), elapsed_ms, 0)


async def ground_node(state: PipelineState) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Ground entities via TerminologyRouter + MedGemma.
    
    Error accumulation: individual entity failures are logged and collected.
    """
    # ... setup code ...
    
    # Parallel grounding with asyncio.gather + semaphore
    semaphore = asyncio.Semaphore(4)
    
    tasks = [
        _ground_entity_parallel(
            entity,
            router,
            entity.get("criterion_text") or entity.get("text", ""),
            idx,
            len(entity_items),
            semaphore,
        )
        for idx, entity in entities_to_ground
    ]
    
    outcomes = await asyncio.gather(*tasks)
    
    # Process outcomes and collect audit entries
    pending_audits: list[tuple[str, dict[str, Any], list[Any], Any]] = []
    for (idx, entity), (result, error, elapsed_ms, retries) in zip(entities_to_ground, outcomes):
        if result is not None:
            pending_audits.append((criterion_id, entity, result.candidates, result))
        else:
            accumulated_errors.append(error_msg)
    
    # Batch-write all audit log entries in a single session
    _flush_audit_logs(protocol_id, pending_audits)
```

**Key Pattern:**
- Uses `asyncio.Semaphore(4)` to limit concurrent API calls
- Dual grounding per entity: `asyncio.gather(TerminologyRouter, OMOP mapper)`
- Error accumulation: failures don't stop processing

**Structure Node (async with parallel processing):**
```python
async def _process_criterion(
    criterion: Criteria,
    protocol_id: str,
    session: Session,
    semaphore: asyncio.Semaphore,
) -> str | None:
    """Process a single criterion: build expression tree.
    
    Args:
        criterion: Criteria record with non-empty field_mappings.
        semaphore: Concurrency limiter for LLM calls.
    
    Returns:
        None on success, error message string on failure.
    """
    async with semaphore:
        try:
            conditions = criterion.conditions or {}
            field_mappings = conditions.get("field_mappings", [])
            
            if not field_mappings:
                return None
            
            tree = await build_expression_tree(
                criterion_text=criterion.text,
                field_mappings=field_mappings,
                criterion_id=criterion.id,
                protocol_id=protocol_id,
                inclusion_exclusion=inclusion_exclusion,
                session=session,
            )
            
            criterion.structured_criterion = tree.model_dump()
            session.add(criterion)
            
            return None
        except Exception as e:
            error_msg = f"Structure build failed for criterion {criterion.id[:12]}: {e}"
            return error_msg
```

### 5. Outbox Processor and Event Loop Management

#### File:
**`/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/libs/events-py/src/events_py/outbox.py`**

```python
class OutboxProcessor:
    """Processes pending outbox events with at-least-once delivery.
    
    Runs handlers in a ThreadPoolExecutor with context propagation.
    """
    
    def __init__(self, engine: Engine, handlers: dict[str, list[Callable]] | None = None):
        self.engine = engine
        self.handlers = handlers or {}
        self.poll_interval = poll_interval
        self.batch_size = batch_size
        # Dedicated thread pool so pipeline handlers don't starve
        self._executor = ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2, thread_name_prefix="outbox")
        self._shutdown_event = asyncio.Event()
    
    def poll_and_process(self) -> int:
        """Poll for pending events and process them.
        
        Handlers are called synchronously within the executor thread.
        """
        processed = 0
        with Session(self.engine) as session:
            events = session.exec(statement).all()
            
            for event in events:
                event_handlers = self.handlers.get(event.event_type, [])
                
                try:
                    for handler in event_handlers:
                        handler(event.payload)  # Sync handler call
                    
                    event.status = "published"
                    session.add(event)
                    processed += 1
                except AuthExpiredError:
                    _mark_event_auth_expired(session, event)
                except Exception:
                    event.retry_count += 1
                    if event.retry_count >= MAX_RETRIES:
                        event.status = "dead_letter"
    
    async def start(self) -> None:
        """Start the polling loop."""
        while not self._shutdown_event.is_set():
            try:
                # Copy context so MLflow ContextVars propagate to the
                # worker thread, avoiding "created in a different Context" warnings
                loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
                ctx = contextvars.copy_context()
                count = await loop.run_in_executor(
                    self._executor, ctx.run, self.poll_and_process
                )
```

**Key Pattern:**
- ThreadPoolExecutor with 2 workers
- Uses `asyncio.get_event_loop().run_in_executor()` with `contextvars.copy_context()`
- Propagates MLflow ContextVars to worker thread
- Handlers run synchronously in thread (sync → async bridge via `asyncio.run()`)

contextvars and produces malformed trace spans.

    call multiple times. Only closes traces older than 1 hour to avoid
    closing currently-running pipelines.
    """

# MLflow Tracing with LangGraph: Comprehensive Research Report

## 1. How MLflow Tracing Works with LangGraph (Autolog Integration)

MLflow's LangGraph tracing is built as an extension of its LangChain integration. You enable it with a single call:

```python
import mlflow
mlflow.langchain.autolog()
```

When enabled, MLflow injects an `MlflowLangchainTracer` as a callback during LangChain/LangGraph inference. This tracer hooks into LangChain's callback system (`on_chain_start`, `on_chain_end`, `on_chat_model_start`, etc.) to automatically capture:

- **One trace per graph invocation** (each `invoke()` or `ainvoke()` call produces a single root trace)
- **Child spans for each node execution** within that trace
- **Sub-spans for LLM calls** within nodes (automatically captured with token usage)
- Input/output data for each span
- Runtime metrics like latency

The trace hierarchy typically looks like:
```
RunnableSequence (root span)
  -> LangGraph (graph span)
    -> Node: "analyze" (node span)
      -> ChatOpenAI (LLM span, with token usage)
    -> Node: "respond" (node span)
      -> ChatOpenAI (LLM span, with token usage)
```

You can further enrich traces by adding manual child spans inside node functions using `mlflow.start_span()`:

```python
from mlflow.entities import SpanType

async def my_node(state):
    with mlflow.start_span(name="validation", span_type=SpanType.TOOL) as span:
        span.set_inputs({"key": "value"})
        # ... validation logic ...
        span.set_outputs("ok")
    return state
```

**Version compatibility**: The autolog integration is compatible with LangChain versions `0.3.19` through `1.2.9` (as of latest docs). MLflow 3.x is strongly recommended for the best tracing experience.

---

## 2. The Known Issue: Single Constantly-Updating Trace / Trace Merging

There are two distinct but related known issues:

### Issue A: Trace Merging with `run_tracer_inline=True`

When `run_tracer_inline=True` is enabled and you call **multiple graph invocations sequentially within the same async function**, the traces can **merge unexpectedly** into a single trace that keeps growing. This happens because the inline tracer maintains context in the main async task, and sequential invocations share that context.

The official documentation explicitly warns:

> "Avoid calling multiple graph invocations sequentially within the same async function, as this may cause traces to merge unexpectedly."

**Workaround**: Wrap each invocation in a separate `asyncio.Task` or use `asyncio.gather()` with individual tasks, so each gets its own async context:

```python
# BAD: sequential invocations in same async function with run_tracer_inline=True
async def process_all():
    await graph.ainvoke(input1)  # trace 1
    await graph.ainvoke(input2)  # may merge into trace 1!

# BETTER: separate tasks
async def process_all():
    await asyncio.gather(
        asyncio.create_task(graph.ainvoke(input1)),
        asyncio.create_task(graph.ainvoke(input2)),
    )
```

### Issue B: Context Divergence in Async (Thread-Based Callback Execution)

Without `run_tracer_inline=True`, LangChain's `AsyncCallbackManager` runs `MlflowLangchainTracer` handlers in a **separate thread** via `copy_context().run()`. The context is copied at the moment of thread creation, but after that the main thread and child thread contexts **diverge**. Any manual spans created in the main thread after the context copy are unaware of spans created in the child thread, resulting in disconnected traces.

This was documented in [GitHub Issue #18216](https://github.com/mlflow/mlflow/issues/18216) and the sequence is:

1. `on_chain_start` event fires
2. `AsyncCallbackManager` copies ContextVar to a new thread, runs `MlflowLangchainTracer.on_chain_start` there
3. The tracer creates a span (`chain_span`), updating ContextVar **in the child thread only**
4. Meanwhile, the main thread's context is unchanged -- any manual spans have no parent linkage

---

## 3. How `mlflow.langchain.autolog()` Interacts with Async LangGraph `ainvoke`

The autolog integration **does support** async functions (`ainvoke`, `abatch`, `astream`), but with important caveats:

**Without `run_tracer_inline=True` (default behavior)**:
- The tracer callback runs in a **thread pool** (offloaded from the main async task)
- Auto-traced spans are created correctly in their own hierarchy
- But **manual `@mlflow.trace` decorators or `mlflow.start_span()` calls** within nodes appear as **separate, disconnected root traces** rather than child spans
- This is because Python's `ContextVar` values diverge between the main async task and the callback thread

**With `run_tracer_inline=True`**:
- The tracer callback runs **in the main async task**
- Manual traces properly nest inside the autolog trace hierarchy
- But the logging operation **is not asynchronous and may block the main thread**
- And sequential invocations in the same async function may merge

**Key limitation still present as of late 2025**: Even with the fix merged in MLflow 3.5.0 for `invoke` (synchronous), the `ainvoke` (async) case still has the thread-divergence issue. As noted by a community member in [Issue #18216](https://github.com/mlflow/mlflow/issues/18216#issuecomment-3432149998) (October 2025): the fix resolved `invoke` but `ainvoke` still produces separate traces when combining manual and automatic tracing, because LangChain's `AsyncCallbackManager` executes the MLflow tracer in a new thread.

---

## 4. The `run_tracer_inline` Parameter

**Full API signature**:
```python
mlflow.langchain.autolog(
    disable=False,
    exclusive=False,
    disable_for_unsupported_versions=False,
    silent=False,
    log_traces=True,
    run_tracer_inline=False,  # <-- the key parameter
)
```

**What `run_tracer_inline` does**:

| `run_tracer_inline` | Tracer Execution | Context Propagation | Risk |
|---------------------|-----------------|---------------------|------|
| `False` (default) | Thread pool (offloaded) | Broken across async boundaries | Manual spans appear as separate traces |
| `True` | Main async task (inline) | Proper propagation | Sequential invocations may merge; may block main thread |

**When to set `True`**:
- You use `@mlflow.trace` decorators or `mlflow.start_span()` inside LangGraph nodes or tools
- You need those manual spans to appear as children of the autolog trace
- You are using async methods like `ainvoke()`

**When to keep `False`**:
- You only use autolog (no manual tracing)
- You make multiple sequential graph invocations in the same async function
- You don't need manual span nesting

---

## 5. Best Practices for MLflow Tracing with Async LangGraph (2025-2026)

Based on the documentation, GitHub issues, and community experience:

### a) Use MLflow 3.5.0+ (ideally 3.6.0+)
Critical fixes for parent span resolution in LangGraph were merged in 3.5.0 ([PR #17273](https://github.com/mlflow/mlflow/pull/17273)).

### b) Enable `run_tracer_inline=True` for async+manual tracing
```python
mlflow.langchain.autolog(run_tracer_inline=True)
```

### c) Isolate concurrent invocations in separate async tasks
```python
# Each invocation gets its own context
tasks = [asyncio.create_task(graph.ainvoke(inp)) for inp in inputs]
results = await asyncio.gather(*tasks)
```

### d) Use `mlflow.config.enable_async_logging()` for production
This makes trace logging non-blocking, reducing overhead by ~80%.

### e) Use `trace_destination` with `context_local=True` for concurrent apps
```python
from mlflow.tracing import set_destination
set_destination(experiment_id="...", context_local=True)
```
This isolates the trace destination per async task or thread, preventing cross-contamination.

### f) Consider `mlflow-tracing` (lightweight SDK) for production
The `mlflow-tracing` package has a 95% smaller footprint than the full `mlflow` package, with full tracing capabilities. Use it when you don't need experiment tracking, model registry, etc.

### g) Flush traces explicitly when needed
```python
mlflow.flush_trace_async_logging()
```

### h) Pass `RunnableConfig` explicitly in Python < 3.11
In Python < 3.11, `asyncio` tasks don't support the `context` parameter, so you must manually pass `config` to `model.ainvoke()`:
```python
async def call_model(state, config):
    response = await model.ainvoke([...], config)
    return {"result": response.content}
```

---

## 6. One Trace Per Invocation vs. One Trace Per Node

**Answer: One trace per graph invocation, with one span per node.**

Each call to `graph.invoke()` or `graph.ainvoke()` produces exactly **one trace** in MLflow. Within that trace, each **node execution** appears as a **child span**. Each LLM call within a node appears as a further nested span.

The structure is hierarchical:
```
Trace (1 per invoke/ainvoke call)
  -> Span: Graph execution
    -> Span: Node "step_1"
      -> Span: ChatOpenAI.invoke
    -> Span: Node "step_2"
      -> Span: ChatOpenAI.invoke
    -> Span: Node "step_3"
```

If you add manual spans inside nodes with `mlflow.start_span()`, they appear as additional children within the node span.

**Exception**: If you mix manual `@mlflow.trace` decorators with autolog in async code without proper configuration, you may get **multiple disconnected root traces** instead of one cohesive trace (the bugs described in Issues #16880 and #18216).

---

## 7. How to Get Real-Time Per-Node Traces

This is the most challenging requirement. The current MLflow behavior:

### Default behavior (traces appear after completion):
- By default, the `MlflowLangchainTracer` collects all spans during execution and logs the complete trace **after the graph invocation finishes**
- In the MLflow UI, the trace appears as a single entry only after the entire graph is done

### MLflow 3.x with OTEL-based real-time span logging:
- MLflow 3.x uses OpenTelemetry (OTEL) format for trace export
- Each span can be exported individually as it completes via the OTEL exporter
- This means spans are **written as they finish**, not batched at the end
- Databricks Unity Catalog supports ingesting these in real-time to Delta tables

### Strategies for real-time per-node visibility:

**Strategy 1: Use `astream_events` with manual span logging**
Instead of `ainvoke`, use LangGraph's `astream_events()` and create manual spans:
```python
async for event in graph.astream_events(input, version="v2"):
    if event["event"] == "on_chain_start":
        # Node started - could log a span start
        pass
    elif event["event"] == "on_chain_end":
        # Node finished - could log a span end
        pass
```

**Strategy 2: Use OpenTelemetry exporter directly**
Configure MLflow to export to an OTEL collector, which processes spans as they arrive:
```python
import mlflow
mlflow.tracing.set_otel_exporter(your_otel_exporter)
```

**Strategy 3: Use `mlflow.start_span()` per node manually (bypass autolog)**
Instead of relying on autolog, manually instrument each node:
```python
@mlflow.trace(name="my_graph")
async def run_graph(input):
    with mlflow.start_span(name="node_1") as span:
        result = await node_1_logic(input)
        span.set_outputs(result)
    # span is closed and could be exported here
    with mlflow.start_span(name="node_2") as span:
        result = await node_2_logic(result)
        span.set_outputs(result)
    return result
```

**Important caveat**: True real-time span-by-span visibility in the MLflow UI is limited. The MLflow tracking server receives spans as they complete (especially with OTEL), but the UI typically shows the complete trace. For real-time monitoring of individual spans as they happen, consider using an OTEL-compatible backend (Jaeger, Zipkin) alongside MLflow.

## Summary of Key GitHub Issues

| Issue | Title | Status | Key Finding |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|
| [#16880](https://github.com/mlflow/mlflow/issues/16880) | Async trace hierarchy flattening with manual + autolog | **Closed** (fixed in 3.5.0) | `@mlflow.trace` + autolog produced separate traces in async; fixed via PR #17273 |
| [#18216](https://github.com/mlflow/mlflow/issues/18216) | Manual + automatic tracing generates multiple traces | **Closed** (fix for invoke, but ainvoke still affected) | Fixed for `invoke` in 3.5.0, but `ainvoke` still has thread-divergence issue per community reports |
| [#12798](https://github.com/mlflow/mlflow/issues/12798) | FR: Tracing for astream_events and invoke | Older FR | Requested LangGraph tracing support |
| [#14533](https://github.com/mlflow/mlflow/issues/14533) | astream_events not streamed with autolog | Bug | Streaming events lost when autolog enabled |
| [#13672](https://github.com/mlflow/mlflow/pull/13672) | Fix LangChain autologging thread-safety | Merged | Thread-safety fix for concurrent tracing |

   110→def _is_auth_expired_error(e: BaseException) -> bool:
   111→    """Return True if the exception is or was caused by credential refresh failure.

   162→        return "Processing timed out"
   163→    if "parse" in error_str:

   214→    LangGraph invocation. asyncio.run() creates an isolated context, so
   215→    we initialize MLflow tracing HERE (inside asyncio.run) rather than
   216→    in the sync caller.
   217→
   218→    Each pipeline node creates its own separate MLflow trace tagged with
   219→    protocol_id, so traces appear in the MLflow UI as they complete
   220→    rather than waiting for the entire pipeline. Filter by the
   221→    protocol_id tag to see all traces from a single pipeline run.
   222→    """
   223→    from protocol_processor.graph import get_graph
   224→
   225→    graph = await get_graph()
   226→
   227→    if _ensure_mlflow():
   228→        import mlflow
   229→
   230→        mlflow.langchain.autolog(run_tracer_inline=True)
   231→        logging.getLogger("mlflow.utils.autologging_utils").setLevel(logging.ERROR)
   232→
   233→    return await graph.ainvoke(initial_state, config)
   234→
   235→
   236→def handle_protocol_uploaded(payload: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   237→    """Handle a ProtocolUploaded event by running the full pipeline.
   238→
   239→    Constructs the initial PipelineState from the event payload and
   240→    invokes the consolidated 5-node LangGraph workflow via asyncio.run().
   241→
   242→    Uses protocol_id as the LangGraph thread_id so that retry_from_checkpoint
   243→    can locate the saved checkpoint by protocol_id alone.
   244→
   245→    Replaces both extraction_service.trigger.handle_protocol_uploaded and
   246→    grounding_service.trigger.handle_criteria_extracted from the v1.x
   247→    two-service architecture.
   248→
   249→    Args:
   250→        payload: Event payload dict containing protocol_id, file_uri, and title.
   251→
   252→    Raises:
   253→        Exception: Re-raised after logging to let the outbox processor
   254→            mark the event as failed for retry.
   255→    """
   256→    protocol_id = payload.get("protocol_id", "unknown")
   257→    logger.info(
   258→        "Handling ProtocolUploaded event for protocol %s (consolidated pipeline)",
   259→        protocol_id,
   260→    )
   261→
   262→    try:
   263→        # Generate unique thread_id per pipeline run to prevent checkpoint collision
   264→        # on re-extraction (same protocol_id would resume old completed checkpoint)
   265→        thread_id = f"{protocol_id}:{uuid4()}"
   266→
   267→        initial_state: dict[str, Any] = {
   268→            "protocol_id": payload["protocol_id"],
   269→            "file_uri": payload["file_uri"],
   270→            "title": payload["title"],
   271→            "batch_id": None,
   272→            "pdf_bytes": None,
   273→            "extraction_json": None,
   274→            "entities_json": None,
   275→            "grounded_entities_json": None,
   276→            "archived_reviewed_criteria": payload.get("archived_reviewed_criteria"),
   277→            "status": "processing",
   278→            "error": None,
   279→            "errors": [],
   280→        }
   281→
   282→        # Store thread_id in protocol metadata for retry_from_checkpoint lookup
   283→        try:
   284→            with Session(engine) as session:
   285→                protocol = session.get(Protocol, protocol_id)
   286→                if protocol:
   287→                    meta = protocol.metadata_ or {}
   288→                    protocol.metadata_ = {
   289→                        **meta,
   290→                        "pipeline_thread_id": thread_id,
   291→                    }
   292→                    session.add(protocol)
   293→                    session.commit()
   294→        except Exception:
   295→            logger.warning(
   296→                "Failed to store pipeline_thread_id for protocol %s",
   297→                protocol_id,
   298→            )
   299→
   300→        config = {"configurable": {"thread_id": thread_id}}
   301→
   302→        asyncio.run(_run_pipeline(initial_state, config, payload))
   303→
   304→        logger.info(
   305→            "Protocol pipeline completed for protocol %s",
   306→            protocol_id,
   307→        )
   308→
   309→    except Exception as e:
   310→        logger.exception(
   311→            "Protocol pipeline failed for protocol %s",
   312→            protocol_id,
   313→        )
   314→        reason = _categorize_pipeline_error(e)
   315→        # Auth expiry: do not retry; outbox marks dead_letter, UI shows login.
   316→        is_auth_expired = _is_auth_expired_error(e)
   317→        category = "auth_expired" if is_auth_expired else "pipeline_failed"
   318→        _update_protocol_failed(
   319→            protocol_id,
   320→            reason,
   321→            category,
   322→            type(e).__name__,
   323→        )
   324→        if is_auth_expired:
   325→            raise AuthExpiredError("Google credentials expired — sign in again") from e
   326→        raise
   327→
   328→
   329→async def retry_from_checkpoint(protocol_id: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
   330→    """Resume pipeline from last checkpoint for a failed protocol.
   331→
   332→    Passes None as the input state to graph.ainvoke, which tells LangGraph
   333→    to resume from the last saved checkpoint for the given thread_id instead
   334→    of starting from scratch. Reads thread_id from protocol.metadata_ where
   335→    it was stored during the original pipeline invocation.
   336→
   337→    Args:
   338→        protocol_id: Protocol ID — used to look up thread_id from metadata.
   339→
   340→    Returns:
   341→        Final pipeline state dict after resuming from checkpoint.
   342→    """
   343→    with Session(engine) as session:
   344→        protocol = session.get(Protocol, protocol_id)
   345→        if not protocol:
   346→            raise ValueError(f"Protocol {protocol_id} not found")
   347→        thread_id = (protocol.metadata_ or {}).get("pipeline_thread_id", protocol_id)
   348→
   349→    from protocol_processor.graph import get_graph
   350→
   351→    graph = await get_graph()
   352→    config = {"configurable": {"thread_id": thread_id}}
   353→    # Pass None as input — LangGraph resumes from last checkpoint
   354→    result = await graph.ainvoke(None, config)
   355→    return result
   356→
```

     1→"""LangGraph workflow definition for the consolidated protocol processor.
     2→
     3→Defines a 7-node StateGraph for the unified protocol processing pipeline:
     4→START -> ingest -> extract -> parse -> ground -> persist
     5→     -> structure -> ordinal_resolve -> END
     6→
     7→Error routing:
     8→- After ingest, extract, parse: conditional edges route to END on error
     9→- After ground: always proceeds to persist (ground uses error accumulation)
    10→- Persist handles partial/total failures internally
    11→- Structure uses error accumulation (same as ground)
    12→- Ordinal resolve uses error accumulation (same pattern)
    13→
    14→Per user decision (v2.0 Architecture): "Flat pipeline with expression tree phase"
    15→"""
    16→
    17→import logging
    18→import os
    19→from typing import Any
    20→
    21→from langgraph.graph import END, START, StateGraph
    22→
    23→from protocol_processor.nodes.extract import extract_node
    24→from protocol_processor.nodes.ground import ground_node
    25→from protocol_processor.nodes.ingest import ingest_node
    26→from protocol_processor.nodes.ordinal_resolve import ordinal_resolve_node
    27→from protocol_processor.nodes.parse import parse_node
    28→from protocol_processor.nodes.persist import persist_node
    29→from protocol_processor.nodes.structure import structure_node
    30→from protocol_processor.state import PipelineState
    31→
    32→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    33→
    34→
    35→def should_continue(state: PipelineState) -> str:
    36→    """Route to error END or continue to next node.
    37→
    38→    Args:
    39→        state: Current pipeline state.
    40→
    41→    Returns:
    42→        'error' if state has a fatal error, 'continue' otherwise.
    43→    """
    44→    return "error" if state.get("error") else "continue"
    45→
    46→
    47→def create_graph(checkpointer: Any = None) -> Any:
    48→    """Create and compile the 7-node protocol processing workflow graph.
    49→
    50→    The graph follows this flow:
    51→    START -> ingest -> extract -> parse -> ground -> persist
    52→         -> structure -> ordinal_resolve -> END
    53→
    54→    Conditional error routing after ingest, extract, and parse.
    55→    Ground always proceeds to persist (ground uses error accumulation).
    56→    Structure and ordinal_resolve use error accumulation (same pattern as ground).
    57→
    58→    Args:
    59→        checkpointer: Optional LangGraph checkpointer for fault-tolerant execution.
    60→            When provided, state is persisted after each node, enabling retry-from-
    61→            checkpoint. Use PostgresSaver for production. Defaults to None (no
    62→            checkpointing).
    63→
    64→    Returns:
    65→        Compiled StateGraph ready for async execution.
    66→    """
    67→    workflow = StateGraph(PipelineState)
    68→
    69→    # Add all 7 nodes
    70→    workflow.add_node("ingest", ingest_node)
    71→    workflow.add_node("extract", extract_node)
    72→    workflow.add_node("parse", parse_node)
    73→    workflow.add_node("ground", ground_node)
    74→    workflow.add_node("persist", persist_node)
    75→    workflow.add_node("structure", structure_node)
    76→    workflow.add_node("ordinal_resolve", ordinal_resolve_node)
    77→
    78→    # Entry point
    79→    workflow.add_edge(START, "ingest")
    80→
    81→    # Conditional error routing after ingest, extract, parse
    82→    for source, target in [
    83→        ("ingest", "extract"),
    84→        ("extract", "parse"),
    85→        ("parse", "ground"),
    86→    ]:
    87→        workflow.add_conditional_edges(
    88→            source,
    89→            should_continue,
    90→            {"continue": target, "error": END},
    91→        )
    92→
    93→    # Ground always proceeds to persist (error accumulation handles partials)
    94→    workflow.add_edge("ground", "persist")
    95→    # Persist proceeds to structure (expression tree building)
    96→    workflow.add_edge("persist", "structure")
    97→    workflow.add_edge("structure", "ordinal_resolve")
    98→    workflow.add_edge("ordinal_resolve", END)
    99→
   100→    return workflow.compile(checkpointer=checkpointer)
   101→
   102→
   103→# Singleton instances for reuse across outbox handler invocations.
   104→# Per Pitfall 1 from research: checkpointer is singleton, created once,
   105→# reused across all invocations. Do NOT create per-invocation.
   106→_graph = None
   107→_checkpointer = None
   108→# Context manager for PostgresSaver; kept entered for app lifetime.
   109→_checkpointer_cm: Any = None
   110→
   111→
   112→async def _get_checkpointer_async() -> Any:
   113→    """Get or create the AsyncPostgresSaver checkpointer singleton.
   114→
   115→    Creates an AsyncPostgresSaver using DATABASE_URL from the environment.
   116→    Uses the async context manager and calls setup() to ensure checkpoint
   117→    tables exist. The context is kept open for the process lifetime.
   118→
   119→    Returns:
   120→        AsyncPostgresSaver instance configured with the application database.
   121→
   122→    Raises:
   123→        KeyError: If DATABASE_URL environment variable is not set.
   124→    """
   125→    global _checkpointer, _checkpointer_cm  # noqa: PLW0603
   126→    if _checkpointer is None:
   127→        from langgraph.checkpoint.postgres.aio import AsyncPostgresSaver
   128→
   129→        db_url = os.environ["DATABASE_URL"]
   130→        _checkpointer_cm = AsyncPostgresSaver.from_conn_string(db_url)
   131→        _checkpointer = await _checkpointer_cm.__aenter__()
   132→        await _checkpointer.setup()  # Create checkpoint tables if they don't exist
   133→    return _checkpointer
   134→
   135→
   136→async def get_graph() -> Any:
   137→    """Get or create the compiled graph singleton.
   138→
   139→    Compiles the graph with an AsyncPostgresSaver checkpointer if DATABASE_URL
   140→    is available. Falls back to no checkpointer if DATABASE_URL is not set
   141→    (e.g. in unit tests).
   142→
   143→    Returns:
   144→        Compiled StateGraph instance.
   145→    """
   146→    global _graph  # noqa: PLW0603
   147→    if _graph is None:
   148→        try:
   149→            checkpointer = await _get_checkpointer_async()
   150→        except Exception:
   151→            # KeyError: DATABASE_URL not set; psycopg.OperationalError: DB
   152→            # unreachable (e.g. unit tests). Compile without checkpointer.
   153→            logger.warning(
   154→                "Failed to create checkpointer — compiling graph without "
   155→                "fault-tolerance. Set DATABASE_URL to enable checkpointing.",
   156→                exc_info=True,
   157→            )
   158→            checkpointer = None
   159→        _graph = create_graph(checkpointer=checkpointer)
   160→    return _graph
   161→
```

     1→"""Extract node: call Gemini with structured output for criteria extraction.
     2→
     3→Thin orchestration node — delegates all LLM logic to the gemini_extractor tool.
     4→Returns extraction_json as a JSON string to minimize LangGraph state size.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→import logging
    10→from typing import Any
    11→
    12→from protocol_processor.state import PipelineState
    13→from protocol_processor.tools.gemini_extractor import extract_criteria_structured
    14→from protocol_processor.tracing import pipeline_span
    15→
    16→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    17→
    18→
    19→async def extract_node(state: PipelineState) -> dict[str, Any]:
    20→    """Extract structured criteria from PDF using Gemini File API.
    21→
    22→    Delegates to extract_criteria_structured tool. Returns a JSON string
    23→    (not dict) to minimize LangGraph state overhead.
    24→
    25→    Args:
    26→        state: Current pipeline state with pdf_bytes, protocol_id, and title.
    27→
    28→    Returns:
    29→        Dict with extraction_json (JSON string), or error dict on failure.
    30→    """
    31→    if state.get("error"):
    32→        return {}
    33→
    34→    protocol_id = state.get("protocol_id", "")
    35→    with pipeline_span(
    36→        "extract_node", span_type="LLM", protocol_id=protocol_id
    37→    ) as span:
    38→        span.set_inputs(
    39→            {
    40→                "protocol_id": state.get("protocol_id", ""),
    41→                "title": state.get("title", ""),
    42→                "pdf_bytes_len": len(state.get("pdf_bytes") or b""),
    43→            }
    44→        )
    45→
    46→        try:
    47→            extraction_json = await extract_criteria_structured(
    48→                pdf_bytes=state["pdf_bytes"],  # type: ignore[arg-type]
    49→                protocol_id=state["protocol_id"],
    50→                title=state["title"],
    51→            )
    52→
    53→            logger.info(
    54→                "Extraction complete for protocol %s",
    55→                state["protocol_id"],
    56→            )
    57→            span.set_outputs(
    58→                {
    59→                    "extraction_json_len": len(extraction_json)
    60→                    if extraction_json
    61→                    else 0,
    62→                }
    63→            )
    64→            return {"extraction_json": extraction_json, "pdf_bytes": None}
    65→
    66→        except Exception as e:
    67→            logger.exception(
    68→                "Extraction failed for protocol %s: %s",
    69→                state.get("protocol_id", "unknown"),
    70→                e,
    71→            )
    72→            span.set_outputs({"error": str(e)})
    73→            return {"error": f"Extraction failed: {e}"}
    74→

     1→"""Ingest node: fetch PDF bytes from GCS or local storage.
     2→
     3→This node is the first in the protocol processing graph. It fetches the PDF
     4→from the file_uri (local:// or gs://) and populates state with raw PDF bytes.
     5→
     6→Architecture note: Graph nodes are integration glue and ARE allowed to import
     7→from api-service for database access (e.g., api_service.storage.engine).
     8→This cross-service import is intentional for workflow orchestration nodes.
     9→"""
    10→
    11→from __future__ import annotations
    12→
    13→import logging
    14→from typing import Any
    15→
    16→from api_service.storage import engine
    17→from shared.models import Protocol
    18→from sqlmodel import Session
    19→
    20→from protocol_processor.state import PipelineState
    21→from protocol_processor.tools.pdf_parser import fetch_pdf_bytes
    22→from protocol_processor.tracing import pipeline_span
    23→
    24→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    25→
    26→
    27→async def ingest_node(state: PipelineState) -> dict[str, Any]:
    28→    """Fetch PDF bytes and update protocol status to 'extracting'.
    29→
    30→    Delegates PDF fetching to the fetch_pdf_bytes tool. Updates the
    31→    protocol record status to 'extracting' in the database.
    32→
    33→    Args:
    34→        state: Current pipeline state with protocol_id, file_uri, and title.
    35→
    36→    Returns:
    37→        Dict with pdf_bytes and status="processing", or error dict on failure.
    38→    """
    39→    if state.get("error"):
    40→        return {}
    41→
    42→    with pipeline_span("ingest_node", protocol_id=state.get("protocol_id", "")) as span:
    43→        span.set_inputs(
    44→            {
    45→                "protocol_id": state.get("protocol_id", ""),
    46→                "file_uri": state.get("file_uri", ""),
    47→            }
    48→        )
    49→
    50→        try:
    51→            pdf_bytes = await fetch_pdf_bytes(state["file_uri"])
    52→
    53→            # Update protocol status to 'extracting'
    54→            with Session(engine) as session:
    55→                protocol = session.get(Protocol, state["protocol_id"])
    56→                if protocol:
    57→                    protocol.status = "extracting"
    58→                    session.add(protocol)
    59→                    session.commit()
    60→
    61→            logger.info(
    62→                "Ingested protocol %s: %d bytes of PDF",
    63→                state["protocol_id"],
    64→                len(pdf_bytes),
    65→            )
    66→            span.set_outputs({"pdf_bytes_len": len(pdf_bytes), "status": "processing"})
    67→            return {"pdf_bytes": pdf_bytes, "status": "processing"}
    68→
    69→        except Exception as e:
    70→            logger.exception(
    71→                "Ingestion failed for protocol %s: %s",
    72→                state.get("protocol_id", "unknown"),
    73→                e,
    74→            )
    75→            span.set_outputs({"error": str(e)})
    76→            return {"error": f"Ingestion failed: {e}", "status": "failed"}
    77→

    38→# Setup basic logging

    40→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

    43→_running_tasks: Set[asyncio.Task] = set()  # noqa: UP006
    44→
    45→
    46→@asynccontextmanager
    47→async def lifespan(app: FastAPI):
    48→    """Manage the lifecycle of the FastAPI application."""
    49→    # Startup
    50→    logger.info("Starting up API service...")
    51→    logger.info("Initializing database...")

    60→        if tracking_uri:
    61→            mlflow.set_tracking_uri(tracking_uri)
    62→            mlflow.set_experiment("protocol-processing")
    63→            # Enable LangChain autolog for extraction/grounding agent traces
    64→            try:
    65→                mlflow.langchain.autolog(run_tracer_inline=True)
    66→                logging.getLogger("mlflow.utils.autologging_utils").setLevel(

    70→            except Exception:

    72→            logger.info(

    76→        else:

    78→    except ImportError:

    80→    except Exception:

    85→
    86→    # Start outbox processor as background task (skip in tests — the poll loop
    87→    # and executor shutdown add seconds of overhead per TestClient instantiation).
    88→    processor = None

    99→        task = asyncio.create_task(processor.start())
   100→        _running_tasks.add(task)

   103→    yield
   104→
   105→    # Shutdown - stop outbox processor first
   106→    if processor:
   107→        await processor.stop()
   108→
   109→    # Wait for background tasks to complete
   110→    if _running_tasks:
   111→        logger.info(f"Waiting for {len(_running_tasks)} tasks to complete...")
   112→        await asyncio.gather(*_running_tasks, return_exceptions=True)
   113→
   114→    engine.dispose()
   115→    logger.info("Shutdown complete")
   116→
   117→

   181→def _check_omop_vocab() -> dict:
   182→    """Check OMOP vocab DB connectivity and that tables are loaded.
   183→
   184→    Returns dict with 'status' ('ok' | 'unavailable' | 'not_loaded')
   185→    and optional 'concept_count'.
   186→    """
   187→    omop_url = os.getenv("OMOP_VOCAB_URL")
   188→    if not omop_url:
   189→        return {"status": "unavailable", "error": "OMOP_VOCAB_URL not set"}
   190→    try:
   191→        omop_engine = sa_create_engine(omop_url, pool_pre_ping=True)
   192→        with omop_engine.connect() as conn:
   193→            row = conn.execute(text("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM concept")).scalar()
   194→            if not row or row == 0:
   195→                return {"status": "not_loaded", "concept_count": 0}
   196→            return {"status": "ok", "concept_count": row}
   197→    except Exception as e:
   198→        return {"status": "unavailable", "error": str(e)}
   199→
   200→
   201→def _check_gcs() -> str:
   202→    """Check GCS connectivity and credential validity.
   203→
   204→    Returns one of: "ok" | "auth_expired" | "unavailable" | "local_storage".
   205→    """
   206→    from api_service.gcs import _use_local_storage, get_bucket_name, get_gcs_client
   207→
   208→    if _use_local_storage():
   209→        return "local_storage"
   210→    try:
   211→        client = get_gcs_client()
   212→        if client is None:
   213→            return "unavailable"
   214→        bucket_name = get_bucket_name()
   215→        client.bucket(bucket_name).exists()
   216→        return "ok"
   217→    except Exception as e:
   218→        try:
   219→            from google.auth.exceptions import RefreshError
   220→
   221→            if isinstance(e, RefreshError):
   222→                return "auth_expired"
   223→        except ImportError:
   224→            pass  # google-auth not installed; skip RefreshError detection
   225→        return "unavailable"
   226→
   227→
   228→def _check_breakers() -> dict:

   230→    from shared.resilience import gcs_breaker, gemini_breaker, vertex_ai_breaker

   232→    return {

     1→"""Outbox processor for publishing pending domain events.
     2→
     3→Implements the transactional outbox pattern with at-least-once delivery.
     4→The OutboxProcessor polls the outbox table for pending events and dispatches
     5→them to registered handlers. The persist_with_outbox helper enables atomic
     6→entity + event writes in a single database transaction.
     7→"""
     8→
     9→from __future__ import annotations
    10→
    11→import asyncio
    12→import contextvars
    13→import logging
    14→from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor
    15→from datetime import datetime, timezone
    16→from typing import Any, Callable
    17→from uuid import uuid4
    18→
    19→from shared.exceptions import AuthExpiredError
    20→from shared.models import OutboxEvent
    21→from sqlalchemy import Engine
    22→from sqlmodel import Session, col, select
    23→
    24→from events_py.models import DomainEventKind
    25→
    26→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    27→
    28→MAX_RETRIES = 3
    29→
    30→AUTH_EXPIRED_REASON = "Google credentials expired — sign in again"
    31→
    32→
    33→def _mark_event_auth_expired(session: Session, event: OutboxEvent) -> None:
    34→    """Mark event as dead_letter due to auth expiry (no retry)."""
    35→    event.retry_count = MAX_RETRIES
    36→    event.status = "dead_letter"
    37→    if event.aggregate_type == "protocol":
    38→        from shared.models import Protocol
    39→
    40→        protocol = session.get(Protocol, event.aggregate_id)
    41→        if protocol:
    42→            protocol.status = "dead_letter"
    43→            protocol.error_reason = AUTH_EXPIRED_REASON
    44→            protocol.metadata_ = {
    45→                **protocol.metadata_,
    46→                "error": {
    47→                    "category": "auth_expired",
    48→                    "reason": AUTH_EXPIRED_REASON,
    49→                    "exception_type": "AuthExpiredError",
    50→                },
    51→            }
    52→            session.add(protocol)
    53→    session.add(event)
    54→    logger.warning(
    55→        "Event %s (type=%s) auth expiry — dead_letter (no retry)",
    56→        event.id,
    57→        event.event_type,
    58→    )
    59→
    60→
    61→class OutboxProcessor:
    62→    """Processes pending outbox events with at-least-once delivery.
    63→
    64→    Usage:
    65→        processor = OutboxProcessor(engine, handlers={
    66→            "protocol_uploaded": [handle_protocol_uploaded],
    67→        })
    68→        await processor.start()  # Runs polling loop
    69→        await processor.stop()   # Graceful shutdown
    70→    """
    71→
    72→    def __init__(
    73→        self,
    74→        engine: Engine,
    75→        handlers: dict[str, list[Callable[..., Any]]] | None = None,
    76→        poll_interval: float = 1.0,
    77→        batch_size: int = 100,
    78→    ) -> None:
    79→        """Initialize the outbox processor.
    80→
    81→        Args:
    82→            engine: SQLAlchemy database engine.
    83→            handlers: Maps event_type string to handler callbacks.
    84→            poll_interval: Seconds between polls.
    85→            batch_size: Max events per poll cycle.
    86→        """
    87→        self.engine = engine
    88→        self.handlers: dict[str, list[Callable[..., Any]]] = handlers or {}
    89→        self.poll_interval = poll_interval
    90→        self.batch_size = batch_size
    91→        # Dedicated thread pool so pipeline handlers don't starve
    92→        # FastAPI's default executor (used for sync endpoints).
    93→        self._executor = ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2, thread_name_prefix="outbox")
    94→        self._shutdown_event = asyncio.Event()
    95→
    96→    def poll_and_process(self) -> int:
    97→        """Poll for pending events and process them.
    98→
    99→        Returns:
   100→            Number of events processed in this poll cycle.
   101→        """
   102→        processed = 0
   103→        with Session(self.engine) as session:
   104→            statement = (
   105→                select(OutboxEvent)
   106→                .where(col(OutboxEvent.status).in_(["pending", "failed"]))
   107→                .order_by(OutboxEvent.created_at.asc())  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
   108→                .limit(self.batch_size)
   109→            )
   110→
   111→            # Use FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED on PostgreSQL
   112→            # for concurrent processor safety
   113→            db_url = str(self.engine.url)
   114→            if "postgresql" in db_url:
   115→                statement = statement.with_for_update(skip_locked=True)
   116→
   117→            events = session.exec(statement).all()
   118→
   119→            for event in events:
   120→                event_handlers = self.handlers.get(event.event_type, [])
   121→
   122→                if not event_handlers:
   123→                    logger.debug(
   124→                        "No handlers for event type: %s",
   125→                        event.event_type,
   126→                    )
   127→
   128→                try:
   129→                    for handler in event_handlers:
   130→                        handler(event.payload)
   131→
   132→                    event.status = "published"
   133→                    event.published_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
   134→                    session.add(event)
   135→                    processed += 1
   136→
   137→                    logger.info(
   138→                        "Published event %s (type=%s)",
   139→                        event.id,
   140→                        event.event_type,
   141→                    )
   142→                except AuthExpiredError:
   143→                    _mark_event_auth_expired(session, event)
   144→                except Exception:
   145→                    event.retry_count += 1
   146→                    if event.retry_count >= MAX_RETRIES:
   147→                        event.status = "dead_letter"
   148→                        logger.warning(
   149→                            "Event %s exhausted retries (%d), marking as dead_letter",
   150→                            event.id,
   151→                            event.retry_count,
   152→                        )
   153→                        # Update protocol status to dead_letter if protocol event
   154→                        if event.aggregate_type == "protocol":
   155→                            from shared.models import Protocol
   156→
   157→                            protocol = session.get(Protocol, event.aggregate_id)
   158→                            if protocol:
   159→                                protocol.status = "dead_letter"
   160→                                protocol.error_reason = "Maximum retries exceeded"
   161→                                protocol.metadata_ = {
   162→                                    **protocol.metadata_,
   163→                                    "error": {
   164→                                        "category": "pipeline_failed",
   165→                                        "reason": "Maximum retries exceeded",
   166→                                        "retry_count": event.retry_count,
   167→                                    },
   168→                                }
   169→                                session.add(protocol)
   170→                    else:
   171→                        event.status = "failed"
   172→
   173→                    session.add(event)
   174→                    logger.exception(
   175→                        "Failed to process event %s (type=%s, retry_count=%d)",
   176→                        event.id,
   177→                        event.event_type,
   178→                        event.retry_count,
   179→                    )
   180→
   181→            session.commit()
   182→
   183→        return processed
   184→
   185→    async def start(self) -> None:
   186→        """Run the polling loop until stop() is called."""
   187→        logger.info(
   188→            "Outbox processor started (interval=%.1fs, batch_size=%d)",
   189→            self.poll_interval,
   190→            self.batch_size,
   191→        )
   192→        while not self._shutdown_event.is_set():
   193→            try:
   194→                # Copy context so MLflow ContextVars propagate to the
   195→                # worker thread, avoiding "created in a different Context"
   196→                # warnings when autologging resets tokens.
   197→                loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
   198→                ctx = contextvars.copy_context()
   199→                count = await loop.run_in_executor(
   200→                    self._executor, ctx.run, self.poll_and_process
   201→                )
   202→                if count > 0:
   203→                    logger.info("Processed %d outbox events", count)
   204→            except Exception:
   205→                logger.exception("Error in outbox processor poll cycle")
   206→            try:
   207→                await asyncio.wait_for(
   208→                    self._shutdown_event.wait(),
   209→                    timeout=self.poll_interval,
   210→                )
   211→            except asyncio.TimeoutError:
   212→                pass
   213→        logger.info("Outbox processor stopped")
   214→
   215→    async def stop(self) -> None:
   216→        """Signal the processor to stop after current poll."""
   217→        logger.info("Outbox processor shutdown requested")
   218→        self._shutdown_event.set()
   219→        self._executor.shutdown(wait=True)
   220→
   221→
   222→def persist_with_outbox(
   223→    session: Session,
   224→    entity: Any,
   225→    event_type: DomainEventKind,
   226→    aggregate_type: str,
   227→    aggregate_id: str,
   228→    payload: dict[str, Any],
   229→    idempotency_key: str | None = None,
   230→) -> OutboxEvent:
   231→    """Persist an entity and its outbox event in the same transaction.
   232→
   233→    The caller is responsible for committing the session.
   234→
   235→    Args:
   236→        session: Active SQLModel session (not yet committed).
   237→        entity: The SQLModel entity to persist.
   238→        event_type: Domain event kind.
   239→        aggregate_type: Type of aggregate (e.g. "protocol").
   240→        aggregate_id: ID of the affected aggregate.
   241→        payload: Event payload dict.
   242→        idempotency_key: Dedup key; auto-generated if omitted.
   243→
   244→    Returns:
   245→        The created OutboxEvent (uncommitted).
   246→    """
   247→    ikey = idempotency_key or (f"{event_type.value}:{aggregate_id}:{uuid4()}")
   248→
   249→    outbox_event = OutboxEvent(
   250→        event_type=event_type.value,
   251→        aggregate_type=aggregate_type,
   252→        aggregate_id=aggregate_id,
   253→        payload=payload,
   254→        idempotency_key=ikey,
   255→        status="pending",
   256→    )
   257→
   258→    session.add(entity)
   259→    session.add(outbox_event)
   260→
   261→    return outbox_event
   262→

     1→"""MLflow request tracing middleware for FastAPI.
     2→
     3→Uses a pure ASGI middleware instead of Starlette's BaseHTTPMiddleware to
     4→preserve contextvars propagation (required for MLflow tracing).
     5→BaseHTTPMiddleware runs call_next in a thread pool, which breaks
     6→contextvars and produces malformed trace spans.
     7→"""
     8→
     9→import logging
    10→import os
    11→import time
    12→
    13→from starlette.types import ASGIApp, Message, Receive, Scope, Send
    14→
    15→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    16→
    17→# Skip tracing for health/readiness probes
    18→_SKIP_PATHS = {"/health", "/ready", "/"}
    19→
    20→
    21→class MLflowRequestMiddleware:
    22→    """Pure ASGI middleware that creates MLflow traces for API requests."""
    23→
    24→    def __init__(self, app: ASGIApp) -> None:
    25→        """Store the ASGI app to call."""
    26→        self.app = app
    27→
    28→    async def __call__(self, scope: Scope, receive: Receive, send: Send) -> None:
    29→        """Handle request and optionally wrap it in an MLflow span."""
    30→        if scope["type"] != "http":
    31→            await self.app(scope, receive, send)
    32→            return
    33→
    34→        path = scope.get("path", "")
    35→        if path in _SKIP_PATHS:
    36→            await self.app(scope, receive, send)
    37→            return
    38→
    39→        tracking_uri = os.getenv("MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI")
    40→        if not tracking_uri:
    41→            await self.app(scope, receive, send)
    42→            return
    43→
    44→        try:
    45→            import mlflow
    46→        except ImportError:
    47→            await self.app(scope, receive, send)
    48→            return
    49→
    50→        method = scope.get("method", "?")
    51→        start = time.perf_counter()
    52→        status_code = 500  # default until we see the actual response
    53→
    54→        # Capture status code from response headers
    55→        async def send_wrapper(message: Message) -> None:
    56→            nonlocal status_code
    57→            if message["type"] == "http.response.start":
    58→                status_code = message.get("status", 500)
    59→            await send(message)
    60→
    61→        app_called = False
    62→        try:
    63→            with mlflow.start_span(
    64→                name=f"{method} {path}",
    65→                span_type="HTTP",
    66→            ) as span:
    67→                query = scope.get("query_string", b"").decode("utf-8", errors="replace")
    68→                span.set_inputs({"method": method, "path": path, "query": query})
    69→
    70→                app_called = True
    71→                await self.app(scope, receive, send_wrapper)
    72→                latency_ms = (time.perf_counter() - start) * 1000
    73→
    74→                span.set_outputs(
    75→                    {
    76→                        "status_code": status_code,
    77→                        "latency_ms": round(latency_ms, 2),
    78→                    }
    79→                )
    80→        except Exception:
    81→            logger.debug(
    82→                "MLflow tracing failed, continuing without trace",
    83→                exc_info=True,
    84→            )
    85→            if not app_called:
    86→                await self.app(scope, receive, send)
    87→

Now I have the complete picture. Here's the root cause analysis and plan.

## Root Cause Analysis

**The problem is `mlflow.langchain.autolog(run_tracer_inline=True)`**, enabled in two places:

1. **`main.py:65`** — in the FastAPI lifespan
2. **`trigger.py:230`** — inside `_run_pipeline()` before `graph.ainvoke()`

Here's what happens:

1. When autolog is active, MLflow injects an `MlflowLangchainTracer` callback that intercepts `graph.ainvoke()`
2. The tracer creates **one root trace for the entire graph invocation** — all 7 nodes live under this single trace
3. Inside each node, `pipeline_span()` calls `mlflow.start_span()` — but with `run_tracer_inline=True`, these become **child spans** of the autolog trace, not separate top-level traces
4. The entire trace is only finalized and fully visible in MLflow **when `ainvoke()` returns** — meaning the whole pipeline must finish before you see anything

The `tracing.py` docstring says "each call creates its own top-level trace" but that was **never true** because autolog was wrapping everything in a parent trace.

## The Fix

**Disable autolog. That's it.** Without autolog, there's no parent trace context during node execution. Each node's `pipeline_span()` → `mlflow.start_span()` will create an **independent root trace** that gets logged to MLflow the moment the node's context manager exits. You'll see traces appearing one-by-one as nodes complete.

Let me implement this and verify it with a test.

```
     1→"""MLflow tracing helpers for pipeline nodes.
     2→
     3→Provides a safe context manager that creates MLflow traces when available
     4→and falls back to a no-op when MLflow is not configured or installed.
     5→
     6→Each node creates its own separate top-level trace (not a child span),
     7→tagged with protocol_id so traces from the same pipeline run can be
     8→grouped and filtered in the MLflow UI.
     9→"""
    10→
    11→from __future__ import annotations
    12→
    13→import logging
    14→import os
    15→from contextlib import contextmanager
    16→from typing import Any
    17→
    18→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    19→
    20→
    21→@contextmanager
    22→def pipeline_span(
    23→    name: str,
    24→    span_type: str = "CHAIN",
    25→    protocol_id: str = "",
    26→):
    27→    """Create a separate MLflow trace for a pipeline node.
    28→
    29→    Each call creates its own top-level trace tagged with protocol_id,
    30→    so individual node traces appear in MLflow as they complete rather
    31→    than waiting for the entire pipeline to finish.
    32→
    33→    Args:
    34→        name: Trace/span name (e.g., "ingest_node", "ground_node").
    35→        span_type: MLflow span type (default "CHAIN").
    36→        protocol_id: Protocol ID to tag the trace with for session grouping.
    37→
    38→    Yields:
    39→        MLflow span object or a no-op wrapper.
    40→    """
    41→    try:
    42→        import mlflow
    43→
    44→        if os.getenv("MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI"):
    45→            with mlflow.start_span(name=name, span_type=span_type) as span:
    46→                if protocol_id:
    47→                    try:
    48→                        mlflow.update_current_trace(
    49→                            tags={"protocol_id": protocol_id},
    50→                        )
    51→                    except Exception:
    52→                        logger.debug("MLflow tag update failed (non-fatal)")
    53→                yield span
    54→                return
    55→    except ImportError:
    56→        logger.debug("mlflow not installed, skipping tracing")
    57→    except Exception:
    58→        logger.debug("MLflow span creation failed, falling back to no-op")
    59→
    60→    # Fallback: no-op span
    61→    yield _NoOpSpan()
    62→
    63→
    64→class _NoOpSpan:
    65→    """No-op span that silently accepts MLflow span API calls."""
    66→
    67→    def set_inputs(self, inputs: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    68→        pass
    69→
    70→    def set_outputs(self, outputs: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    71→        pass
    72→
    73→    def set_status(self, status: str) -> None:
    74→        pass
    75→
```

     9→from __future__ import annotations
    10→
    11→from protocol_processor.state import PipelineState
    12→
    13→
    14→def _make_initial_state(**overrides) -> dict:
    15→    """Build a minimal PipelineState-compatible dict."""
    16→    base: dict = {
    17→        "protocol_id": "test-protocol-id",
    18→        "file_uri": "local://test.pdf",

    34→class TestCreateGraph:

    46→        from protocol_processor.graph import create_graph
    47→
    48→        graph = create_graph()
    49→        # LangGraph adds __start__ and __end__ internal nodes
    50→        # User nodes are those without leading underscores
    51→        all_nodes = graph.nodes
    52→        user_nodes = {name for name in all_nodes if not name.startswith("__")}
    53→        assert user_nodes == {

    57→            "ground",

    60→            "ordinal_resolve",

    62→

### Start a Trace with MlflowClient

Source: https://mlflow.org/docs/latest/api_reference/python_api/mlflow.client

This function, `start_trace`, allows for the imperative creation of a new MLflow trace and its root span. It offers granular control over trace and span properties like name, type, inputs, attributes, and association with experiments. It's essential to call `end_trace` to finalize and record traces initiated this way.

```python
from mlflow import MlflowClient

client = MlflowClient()

# Start a trace named 'my_trace'
root_span = client.start_trace("my_trace")
trace_id = root_span.trace_id

# You can also specify span type, inputs, attributes, tags, and experiment_id
# root_span = client.start_trace("my_trace", span_type='MY_SPAN_TYPE', inputs={'input_data': 'value'}, attributes={'key': 'value'}, tags={'tag_key': 'tag_value'}, experiment_id='12345')

# Remember to end the trace later
# client.end_trace(trace_id)
```

--------------------------------

### MLflow Client Trace and Span Management

Source: https://mlflow.org/docs/latest/api_reference/python_api/mlflow.client

This section covers the imperative APIs for manually creating and managing traces and spans using the MlflowClient.

```APIDOC
## POST /mlflow/client/start_trace

### Description
Creates a new trace object and starts a root span under it. This is an imperative API for manually creating spans, unlike higher-level APIs that manage span lifecycles automatically. A trace started with this method must be ended by calling `MlflowClient().end_trace(trace_id)` to ensure it's recorded.

### Method
POST

### Endpoint
/mlflow/client/start_trace

### Parameters
#### Query Parameters
- **name** (str) - Required - The name of the trace (and the root span).
- **span_type** (str) - Optional - The type of the span. Defaults to 'UNKNOWN'.
- **inputs** (Any) - Optional - Inputs to set on the root span of the trace.
- **attributes** (dict[str, str]) - Optional - A dictionary of attributes to set on the root span of the trace.
- **tags** (dict[str, str]) - Optional - A dictionary of tags to set on the trace.
- **experiment_id** (str) - Optional - The ID of the experiment to create the trace in. If not provided, MLflow will determine the experiment based on context (e.g., `mlflow.set_experiment()`, environment variables, or default experiment).
- **start_time_ns** (int) - Optional - The start time of the trace in nanoseconds since the UNIX epoch.

### Request Example
```json
{
  "name": "my_trace",
  "span_type": "custom",
  "inputs": {"key": "value"},
  "attributes": {"attr1": "val1"},
  "tags": {"tag1": "tval1"},
  "experiment_id": "12345",
  "start_time_ns": 1678886400000000000
}
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **span** (Span) - An `Span` object representing the root span of the trace.

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "span": {
    "trace_id": "a1b2c3d4e5f6",
    "span_id": "f6e5d4c3b2a1",
    "name": "my_trace",
    "start_time_ns": 1678886400000000000,
    "end_time_ns": null,
    "inputs": {"key": "value"},
    "outputs": null,
    "attributes": {"attr1": "val1"},
    "status": "RUNNING",
    "span_type": "custom"
  }
}
```

## POST /mlflow/client/start_span

### Description
Creates a child span associated with a specific trace and parent span. This method is typically used within the context of a manually managed trace started with `start_trace()`.

### Method
POST

### Endpoint
/mlflow/client/start_span

### Parameters
#### Query Parameters
- **name** (str) - Required - The name of the child span.
- **trace_id** (str) - Required - The ID of the trace to which this span belongs.
- **parent_id** (str) - Required - The ID of the parent span.
- **inputs** (dict) - Optional - Inputs to set on the child span.
- **start_time_ns** (int) - Optional - The start time of the span in nanoseconds since the UNIX epoch.

### Request Example
```json
{
  "name": "child_span",
  "trace_id": "a1b2c3d4e5f6",
  "parent_id": "f6e5d4c3b2a1",
  "inputs": {"x": 10},
  "start_time_ns": 1678886410000000000
}
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **span** (Span) - An `Span` object representing the newly created child span.

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "span": {
    "trace_id": "a1b2c3d4e5f6",
    "span_id": "1a2b3c4d5e6f",
    "name": "child_span",
    "start_time_ns": 1678886410000000000,
    "end_time_ns": null,
    "inputs": {"x": 10},
    "outputs": null,
    "attributes": {},
    "status": "RUNNING",
    "span_type": "UNKNOWN"
  }
}
```

## POST /mlflow/client/end_span

### Description
Ends a span, recording its completion time, attributes, and outputs. This method should be called after a span has finished its execution.

### Method
POST

### Endpoint
/mlflow/client/end_span

### Parameters
#### Query Parameters
- **trace_id** (str) - Required - The ID of the trace the span belongs to.
- **span_id** (str) - Required - The ID of the span to end.
- **attributes** (dict[str, str]) - Optional - A dictionary of attributes to set on the span upon completion.
- **outputs** (dict) - Optional - Outputs to set on the span upon completion.
- **end_time_ns** (int) - Optional - The end time of the span in nanoseconds since the UNIX epoch.

### Request Example
```json
{
  "trace_id": "a1b2c3d4e5f6",
  "span_id": "1a2b3c4d5e6f",
  "attributes": {"factor": 2},
  "outputs": {"y": 100},
  "end_time_ns": 1678886420000000000
}
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **status** (str) - Indicates the success of the operation (e.g., "OK").

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "status": "OK"
}
```

## POST /mlflow/client/end_trace

### Description
Ends a trace, marking it as complete. This method must be called for any trace that was started using `client.start_trace()` to ensure the trace is recorded.

### Method
POST

### Endpoint
/mlflow/client/end_trace

### Parameters
#### Query Parameters
- **trace_id** (str) - Required - The ID of the trace to end.

### Request Example
```json
{
  "trace_id": "a1b2c3d4e5f6"
}
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **status** (str) - Indicates the success of the operation (e.g., "OK").

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "status": "OK"
}
```
```
