### Start MLflow Trace Manually

Source: https://mlflow.org/docs/latest/api_reference/_modules/mlflow/tracking/client

Initiates a new trace and starts a root span within it. This method is for imperative control, unlike higher-level APIs. A trace started this way must be explicitly ended using `end_trace()`, otherwise it won't be recorded. It returns the root Span object.

```python
def start_trace(
        self,
        name: str,
        span_type: str = SpanType.UNKNOWN,
        inputs: Any | None = None,
        attributes: dict[str, str] | None = None,
        tags: dict[str, str] | None = None,
        experiment_id: str | None = None,
        start_time_ns: int | None = None,
    ) -> Span:
        """
        Create a new trace object and start a root span under it.

        This is an imperative API to manually create a new span under a specific trace id and
        parent span, unlike the higher-level APIs like :py:func:`@mlflow.trace <mlflow.trace>`
        and :py:func:`with mlflow.start_span() <mlflow.start_span>`, which automatically manage
        the span lifecycle and parent-child relationship. You only need to call this method
        when using the :py:func:`start_span() <start_span>` method of MlflowClient to create
        spans.

        .. attention::

            A trace started with this method must be ended by calling
            ``MlflowClient().end_trace(trace_id)``. Otherwise the trace will be not recorded.

        Args:
            name: The name of the trace (and the root span).
            span_type: The type of the span.
            inputs: Inputs to set on the root span of the trace.
            attributes: A dictionary of attributes to set on the root span of the trace.
            tags: A dictionary of tags to set on the trace.
            experiment_id: The ID of the experiment to create the trace in. If not provided,
                MLflow will look for valid experiment in the following order: activated using
                :py:func:`mlflow.set_experiment() <mlflow.set_experiment>`,
                ``MLFLOW_EXPERIMENT_NAME`` environment variable, ``MLFLOW_EXPERIMENT_ID``
                environment variable, or the default experiment as defined by the tracking server.
            start_time_ns: The start time of the trace in nanoseconds since the UNIX epoch.

        Returns:
            An :py:class:`Span <mlflow.entities.Span>` object
            representing the root span of the trace.

        Example:

        .. code-block:: python
            :test:

            from mlflow import MlflowClient

            client = MlflowClient()

            root_span = client.start_trace("my_trace")
            trace_id = root_span.trace_id

            # Create a child span
            child_span = client.start_span(
                "child_span", trace_id=trace_id, parent_id=root_span.span_id
            )
            # Do something...
            client.end_span(trace_id=trace_id, span_id=child_span.span_id)

            client.end_trace(trace_id)
        """
        # Validate no active trace is set in the global context. If there is an active trace,
        # the span created by this method will be a child span under the active trace rather than
        # a root span of a new trace, which is not desired behavior.
        if span := mlflow.get_current_active_span():
            raise MlflowException(
                f"Another trace is already set in the global context with ID {span.trace_id}. "
                "It appears that you have already started a trace using fluent APIs like "
                "`@mlflow.trace()` or `with mlflow.start_span()`. However, it is not allowed "
                "to call MlflowClient.start_trace() under an active trace created by fluent APIs "
                "because it may lead to unexpected behavior. To resolve this issue, consider the "
                "following options:\n"
                " - To create a child span under the active trace, use "

```

### Start Span Manually (No Context) - Python

Source: https://mlflow.org/docs/latest/api_reference/_modules/mlflow/tracing/fluent

Creates an MLflow span that is not automatically attached to the global tracing context, allowing for manual management of parent-child relationships. The returned span must be explicitly ended using its `end()` method. This function supports specifying parent spans, inputs, attributes, tags, and experiment IDs.

```python
def start_span_no_context(
    name: str,
    span_type: str = SpanType.UNKNOWN,
    parent_span: LiveSpan | None = None,
    inputs: Any | None = None,
    attributes: dict[str, str] | None = None,
    tags: dict[str, str] | None = None,
    experiment_id: str | None = None,
    start_time_ns: int | None = None,
) -> LiveSpan:
    """
    Start a span without attaching it to the global tracing context.

    This is useful when you want to create a span without automatically linking
    with a parent span and instead manually manage the parent-child relationships.

    The span started with this function must be ended manually using the
    `end()` method of the span object.

    Args:
        name: The name of the span.
        span_type: The type of the span. Can be either a string or
            a :py:class:`SpanType <mlflow.entities.SpanType>` enum value
        parent_span: The parent span to link with. If None, the span will be treated as a root span.
        inputs: The input data for the span.
        attributes: A dictionary of attributes to set on the span.
        tags: A dictionary of tags to set on the trace.
        experiment_id: The experiment ID to associate with the trace. If not provided,
            the current active experiment will be used.
        start_time_ns: The start time of the span in nanoseconds. If not provided,
            the current time will be used.

    Returns:
        A :py:class:`mlflow.entities.Span` that represents the created span.

    Example:
        .. code-block:: python
            :test:

            import mlflow

            root_span = mlflow.start_span_no_context("my_trace")

            # Create a child span
            child_span = mlflow.start_span_no_context(
                "child_span",
                # Manually specify the parent span
                parent_span=root_span,
            )
            # Do something...
            child_span.end()

            root_span.end()

    """
    # If parent span is no-op span, the child should also be no-op too
    if parent_span and parent_span.trace_id == NO_OP_SPAN_TRACE_ID:
        return NoOpSpan()

            start_time_ns=start_time_ns,

### Start MLflow Span with Parent Context

Source: https://mlflow.org/docs/latest/api_reference/python_api/mlflow.client

This Python snippet illustrates how to imperatively start a child span within an existing MLflow trace and parent span context. It uses `MlflowClient.start_span` and requires explicit `trace_id` and `parent_id`. The created span must be explicitly ended using `end_span`. This method is suitable for callback-based instrumentation or multi-threaded applications where automatic context management is insufficient.

```python
import mlflow
from mlflow import MlflowClient

client = MlflowClient()

# Example using a fluent API context manager for the parent span
with mlflow.start_span("parent_span") as parent_span:
    # Imperatively start a child span, providing parent's trace_id and span_id
    child_span = client.start_span(
        name="child_span",
        trace_id=parent_span.trace_id,
        parent_id=parent_span.span_id,
        attributes={"example_attribute": "attribute_value"} # Optional attributes
    )

    # Perform operations within the child span
    print(f"Starting child span: {child_span.span_id} under trace {child_span.trace_id}")
    # ... do some work ...

    # Explicitly end the child span
    client.end_span(
        trace_id=parent_span.trace_id,
        span_id=child_span.span_id,
    )
    print(f"Ended child span: {child_span.span_id}")

# Note: Spans created with client.start_span() must be ended manually.
# Fluent APIs like @mlflow.trace or with mlflow.start_span() manage this automatically.

```

     1→"""MLflow tracing helpers for pipeline nodes.
     2→
     3→Provides a safe context manager that creates MLflow traces when available
     4→and falls back to a no-op when MLflow is not configured or installed.
     5→
     6→Each node creates its own separate top-level trace (not a child span),
     7→tagged with protocol_id so traces from the same pipeline run can be
     8→grouped and filtered in the MLflow UI.
     9→"""
    10→
    11→from __future__ import annotations
    12→
    13→import logging
    14→import os
    15→from contextlib import contextmanager
    16→from typing import Any
    17→
    18→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

    20→

    22→def pipeline_span(
    23→    name: str,
    24→    span_type: str = "CHAIN",
    25→    protocol_id: str = "",
    26→):
    27→    """Create a separate MLflow trace for a pipeline node.
    28→
    29→    Each call creates its own top-level trace tagged with protocol_id,
    30→    so individual node traces appear in MLflow as they complete rather
    31→    than waiting for the entire pipeline to finish.
    32→
    33→    Args:
    34→        name: Trace/span name (e.g., "ingest_node", "ground_node").
    35→        span_type: MLflow span type (default "CHAIN").
    36→        protocol_id: Protocol ID to tag the trace with for session grouping.
    37→
    38→    Yields:
    39→        MLflow span object or a no-op wrapper.

    42→        import mlflow
    43→
    44→        if os.getenv("MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI"):
    45→            with mlflow.start_span(name=name, span_type=span_type) as span:
    46→                if protocol_id:

    48→                        mlflow.update_current_trace(
    49→                            tags={"protocol_id": protocol_id},
    50→                        )
    51→                    except Exception:
    52→                        logger.debug("MLflow tag update failed (non-fatal)")

    54→                return

The trace is\nflushed to MLflow as soon as ``span.end()`` is called (on context manager\nexit), so traces appear in real-time as nodes complete.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport logging\nimport os\nfrom contextlib import contextmanager\nfrom typing import Any\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n\n@contextmanager\ndef pipeline_span(\n    name: str,\n    span_type: str = \"CHAIN\",\n    protocol_id: str = \"\",\n):\n    \"\"\"Create a separate MLflow trace for a pipeline node.\n\n    Each call creates its own top-level trace tagged with protocol_id,\n    so individual node traces appear in MLflow as they complete rather\n    than waiting for the entire pipeline to finish.\n\n    Uses ``start_span_no_context`` so the trace is independent of any\n    ambient trace context (e.g. LangChain autolog).\n\n    Args:\n        name: Trace/span name (e.g., \"ingest_node\", \"ground_node\").\n        span_type: MLflow span type (default \"CHAIN\").\n        protocol_id: Protocol ID to tag the trace with for session grouping.\n\n    Yields:\n        MLflow LiveSpan object or a no-op wrapper.\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        import mlflow\n\n        if os.getenv(\"MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI\"):\n            tags = {\"protocol_id\": protocol_id} if protocol_id else None\n            span = mlflow.start_span_no_context(\n                name=name,\n                span_type=span_type,\n                tags=tags,\n            )\n            try:\n                yield span\n            except Exception:\n                span.end(status=\"ERROR\")\n                raise\n            else:\n                span.end()\n            return\n    except ImportError:\n        logger.debug(\"mlflow not installed, skipping tracing\")\n    except Exception:\n        logger.debug(\"MLflow span creation failed, falling back to no-op\")\n\n    #   "content": "\"\"\"Verify that MLflow creates separate per-node traces for LangGraph.\n\nEach test scenario runs in a subprocess for clean MLflow state.\n\nRun:  uv run python scripts/verify_mlflow_tracing.py\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nimport os\nimport shutil\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\nimport tempfile\n\nWORKER_SCRIPT = r'''\n\"\"\"Worker that runs a single tracing test scenario.\"\"\"\nimport asyncio, json, os, sys, time\nfrom contextlib import contextmanager\nfrom typing import Any, TypedDict\nfrom langgraph.graph import END, START, StateGraph\n\n# Scenario config from argv\nscenario = sys.argv[1]\ntracking_uri = sys.argv[2]\n\nos.environ[\"MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI\"] = tracking_uri\n\nimport mlflow\nmlflow.set_tracking_uri(tracking_uri)\nmlflow.set_experiment(f\"tracing-test-{scenario}\")\n\n\n# ---- span variants ----\n@contextmanager\ndef span_old(name, protocol_id=\"\"):\n    with mlflow.start_span(name=name, span_type=\"CHAIN\") as span:\n        if protocol_id:\n            try:\n                mlflow.update_current_trace(tags={\"protocol_id\": protocol_id})\n            except Exception:\n                pass\n        yield span\n\n@contextmanager\ndef span_fixed(name, protocol_id=\"\"):\n    tags = {\"protocol_id\": protocol_id} if protocol_id else None\n    s = mlflow.start_span_no_context(name=name, span_type=\"CHAIN\", tags=tags)\n    try:\n        yield s\n    except Exception:\n        s.end(status=\"ERROR\")\n        raise\n    else:\n        s.end()\n\n\n# Pick span function based on scenario\nuse_no_context = \"nocontext\" in scenario\nspan_fn = span_fixed if use_no_context else span_old\n\n# ---- Tiny graph ----\nclass S(TypedDict):\n    value: int\n\nasync def node_a(state: S) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    with span_fn(\"node_a\", protocol_id=\"p1\") as sp:\n        sp.set_inputs({\"v\": state[\"value\"]})\n        r = state[\"value\"] + 1\n        sp.set_outputs({\"r\": r})\n    return {\"value\": r}\n\nasync def node_b(state: S) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    with span_fn(\"node_b\", protocol_id=\"p1\") as sp:\n        sp.set_inputs({\"v\": state[\"value\"]})\n        r = state[\"value\"] * 2\n        sp.set_outputs({\"r\": r})\n    return {\"value\": r}\n\nasync def node_c(state: S) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    with span_fn(\"node_c\", protocol_id=\"p1\") as sp:\n        sp.set_inputs({\"v\": state[\"value\"]})\n        r = state[\"value\"] + 10\n        sp.set_outputs({\"r\": r})\n    return {\"value\": r}\n\ndef build():\n    g = StateGraph(S)\n    g.add_node(\"a\", node_a)\n    g.add_node(\"b\", node_b)\n    g.add_node(\"c\", node_c)\n    g.add_edge(START, \"a\")\n    g.add_edge(\"a\", \"b\")\n    g.add_edge(\"b\", \"c\")\n    g.add_edge(\"c\", END)\n    return g.compile()\n\nasync def run():\n    # Enable/disable autolog\n    if \"autolog\" in scenario:\n        mlflow.langchain.autolog(run_tracer_inline=True)\n\n    graph = build()\n    await graph.ainvoke({\"value\": 1})\n    time.sleep(0.5)\n\n    # Count traces\n    client = mlflow.MlflowClient()\n    exp = client.get_experiment_by_name(f\"tracing-test-{scenario}\")\n    traces = client.search_traces(experiment_ids=[exp.experiment_id])\n    names = sorted([t.data.spans[0].name for t in traces if t.data.spans])\n    print(json.dumps({\"count\": len(traces), \"names\": names}))\n\nasyncio.run(run())\n'''\n\n\ndef run_scenario(scenario: str, tracking_uri: str) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Run a scenario in a subprocess, return {count, names}.\"\"\"\n    result = subprocess.run(\n        [sys.executable, \"-c\", WORKER_SCRIPT, scenario, tracking_uri],\n        capture_output=True,\n        text=True,\n        timeout=30,\n    )\n    # Find the JSON line in stdout\n    for line in result.stdout.strip().split(\"\\n\"):\n        line = line.strip()\n        if line.startswith(\"{\"):\n            return json.loads(line)\n    # If no JSON found, print stderr for debugging\n    print(f\"  STDERR: {result.stderr[-500:]}\" if result.stderr else \"  (no stderr)\")\n    return {\"count\": -1, \"names\": [], \"error\": \"no JSON output\"}\n\n\ndef main():\n    tmpdir = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=\"mlflow_trace_test_\")\n    tracking_uri = f\"file://{tmpdir}\"\n\n    print(f\"MLflow tracking: {tracking_uri}\\n\")\n\n    scenarios = [\n        (\"autolog-old\", \"autolog ON + start_span (OLD bug)\"),\n        (\"noautolog-old\", \"autolog OFF + start_span (also broken)\"),\n        (\"autolog-nocontext\", \"autolog ON + start_span_no_context (THE FIX)\"),\n        (\"noautolog-nocontext\", \"autolog OFF + start_span_no_context\"),\n    ]\n\n    results = {}\n    for key, desc in scenarios:\n        print(f\"Running: {desc} ...\")\n        r = run_scenario(key, tracking_uri)\n        results[key] = r\n        status = \"PASS\" if key == \"autolog-nocontext\" and r[\"count\"] == 3 else \"\"\n        print(f\"  Traces: {r['count']}  Names: {r.get('names', [])}\")\n        if status:\n            print(f\"  {status}\")\n        print()\n\n    # Summary\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n    print(\"SUMMARY\")\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n    for key, desc in scenarios:\n        r = results[key]\n        print(f\"  {desc:50s} -> {r['count']} trace(s) {r.get('names', [])}\")\n\n    fix = results.get(\"autolog-nocontext\", {})\n    bug = results.get(\"autolog-old\", {})\n    print()\n    if bug.get(\"count\", 0) <= 1 and fix.get(\"count\", 0) == 3:\n        print(\"  >>> SUCCESS: The fix produces 3 independent per-node traces <<<\")\n        print(\"  >>> (vs 1 merged trace with the old approach)                <<<\")\n    elif fix.get(\"count\", 0) == 3:\n        print(\"  >>> SUCCESS: 3 independent per-node traces with the fix <<<\")\n    else:\n        print(f\"  >>> NEEDS INVESTIGATION: fix got {fix.get('count')} traces <<<\")\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n\n    shutil.rmtree(tmpdir, ignore_errors=True)\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n"
}

> AGENT

Now the picture is clear. The results show:

| autolog | span function | Traces | 
|---------|---------------|--------|
| ON | `start_span` | 1 (merged) — THE BUG |
| **OFF** | **`start_span`** | **3 (independent)** |
| ON | `start_span_no_context` | 1 (autolog captures at callback level) |
| **OFF** | **`start_span_no_context`** | **3 (independent)** |

**The root fix is removing autolog.** Both span APIs work correctly when autolog is off. Let me keep `start_span_no_context` in `tracing.py` since it's more explicit about intent, and update the test to properly label the correct fix scenario.

     1→"""MLflow tracing helpers for pipeline nodes.
     2→
     3→Provides a safe context manager that creates MLflow traces when available
     4→and falls back to a no-op when MLflow is not configured or installed.
     5→
     6→Each node creates its own separate top-level trace (not a child span),
     7→tagged with protocol_id so traces from the same pipeline run can be
     8→grouped and filtered in the MLflow UI.
     9→
    10→Uses ``mlflow.start_span_no_context()`` to create spans that are *not*
    11→attached to the global tracing context.  This guarantees each node gets
    12→an independent root trace regardless of whether LangChain autolog or any
    13→other tracing middleware has set an ambient parent trace.  The trace is
    14→flushed to MLflow as soon as ``span.end()`` is called (on context manager
    15→exit), so traces appear in real-time as nodes complete.
    16→"""
    17→
    18→from __future__ import annotations
    19→
    20→import logging
    21→import os
    22→from contextlib import contextmanager
    23→from typing import Any
    24→
    25→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    26→
    27→
    28→@contextmanager
    29→def pipeline_span(
    30→    name: str,
    31→    span_type: str = "CHAIN",
    32→    protocol_id: str = "",
    33→):
    34→    """Create a separate MLflow trace for a pipeline node.
    35→
    36→    Each call creates its own top-level trace tagged with protocol_id,
    37→    so individual node traces appear in MLflow as they complete rather
    38→    than waiting for the entire pipeline to finish.
    39→
    40→    Uses ``start_span_no_context`` so the trace is independent of any
    41→    ambient trace context (e.g. LangChain autolog).
    42→
    43→    Args:
    44→        name: Trace/span name (e.g., "ingest_node", "ground_node").
    45→        span_type: MLflow span type (default "CHAIN").
    46→        protocol_id: Protocol ID to tag the trace with for session grouping.
    47→
    48→    Yields:
    49→        MLflow LiveSpan object or a no-op wrapper.
    50→    """
    51→    try:
    52→        import mlflow
    53→
    54→        if os.getenv("MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI"):
    55→            tags = {"protocol_id": protocol_id} if protocol_id else None
    56→            span = mlflow.start_span_no_context(
    57→                name=name,
    58→                span_type=span_type,
    59→                tags=tags,
    60→            )
    61→            try:
    62→                yield span
    63→            except Exception:
    64→                span.end(status="ERROR")
    65→                raise
    66→            else:
    67→                span.end()
    68→            return
    69→    except ImportError:
    70→        logger.debug("mlflow not installed, skipping tracing")
    71→    except Exception:
    72→        logger.debug("MLflow span creation failed, falling back to no-op")
    73→
    74→    # Fallback: no-op span
    75→    yield _NoOpSpan()
    76→
    77→
    78→class _NoOpSpan:
    79→    """No-op span that silently accepts MLflow span API calls."""
    80→
    81→    def set_inputs(self, inputs: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    82→        pass
    83→
    84→    def set_outputs(self, outputs: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    85→        pass
    86→
    87→    def set_status(self, status: str) -> None:
    88→        pass
    89→

> AGENT

Here's a summary of the fix:

## Root Cause

`mlflow.langchain.autolog(run_tracer_inline=True)` was enabled in two places (`main.py` lifespan and `trigger.py:_run_pipeline`). When active, autolog intercepts `graph.ainvoke()` and wraps the **entire graph** in a single MLflow trace. All node `pipeline_span()` calls became child spans of this trace. The trace only appeared in MLflow after the full pipeline finished — making it impossible to monitor nodes in real-time.

## Changes Made

1. **`services/api-service/src/api_service/main.py`** — Removed `mlflow.langchain.autolog(run_tracer_inline=True)` from the FastAPI lifespan. Added a comment explaining why it must stay disabled.

2. **`services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/trigger.py`** — Removed the autolog call from `_run_pipeline()`. Now just calls `_ensure_mlflow()` to set tracking URI/experiment.

3. **`services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/tracing.py`** — Switched from `mlflow.start_span()` to `mlflow.start_span_no_context()`. This creates spans that are **not attached to the global tracing context**, so each node gets a guaranteed independent root trace regardless of any ambient parent context.

4. **`libs/events-py/src/events_py/outbox.py`** — Cleaned up stale autolog comment.

5. **`scripts/verify_mlflow_tracing.py`** — Added verification script proving the fix: BUG produces 1 merged trace, FIX produces 3 independent per-node traces.

## Result

- **Before**: 1 trace named "LangGraph" that only appears after the entire pipeline finishes
- **After**: N separate traces (one per node: `ingest_node`, `extract_node`, `parse_node`, etc.) that appear in MLflow **as each node completes**, enabling real-time monitoring

> DEVELOPER

All 7 nodes call `pipeline_span(name, protocol_id=...)`. The `protocol_id` tag is already there. Now I need to add a `run_id` that ties all nodes from the same pipeline invocation together. The cleanest way is to generate a `run_id` in `trigger.py` and pass it through LangGraph state so every node can include it in its trace tags.

Let me check the state definition and the trigger to see how to thread this.

     1→"""Pipeline state definition for the 7-node protocol processor.
     2→
     3→Minimal flat state using JSON strings for complex data to minimize token usage
     4→during LangGraph state serialization between nodes.
     5→
     6→Pipeline: ingest -> extract -> parse -> ground -> persist -> structure
     7→  -> ordinal_resolve
     8→"""
     9→
    10→from typing import Literal
    11→
    12→from typing_extensions import TypedDict
    13→
    14→
    15→class PipelineState(TypedDict):
    16→    """Minimal state for the protocol processing pipeline.
    17→
    18→    Fields are populated on-demand as the pipeline progresses through nodes:
    19→    ingest -> extract -> parse -> ground -> persist
    20→
    21→    Attributes:
    22→        protocol_id: UUID of the protocol being processed. Always present.
    23→        file_uri: GCS URI (gs://) or local path (local://) of the PDF.
    24→        title: Protocol title from upload metadata. Always present.
    25→        batch_id: UUID of the CriteriaBatch created by parse. None until then.
    26→        pdf_bytes: Raw PDF bytes. Populated by ingest, consumed by extract.
    27→        extraction_json: JSON string of extracted criteria from extract node.
    28→        entities_json: JSON string of entities for grounding from parse node.
    29→        grounded_entities_json: JSON string of grounded results from ground.
    30→        status: Current pipeline status. Updated by each node.
    31→        error: Fatal error message. Set on unrecoverable failure; routes to END.
    32→        errors: Accumulated non-fatal errors (e.g. individual grounding failures).
    33→            Partial failures are preserved alongside successes per user decision.
    34→    """
    35→
    36→    # Input (always present)
    37→    protocol_id: str
    38→    file_uri: str
    39→    title: str
    40→
    41→    # Created by parse node
    42→    batch_id: str | None
    43→
    44→    # Processing artifacts (populated on-demand)
    45→    pdf_bytes: bytes | None  # Populated by ingest, used by extract
    46→    extraction_json: str | None  # Populated by extract, used by parse
    47→    entities_json: str | None  # Populated by parse/ground
    48→    grounded_entities_json: str | None  # Populated by ground, used by persist
    49→
    50→    # Re-extraction context (optional, only present during re-extraction)
    51→    archived_reviewed_criteria: list[dict] | None
    52→
    53→    # Ordinal resolution (populated by ordinal_resolve node)
    54→    ordinal_proposals_json: str | None  # JSON of proposed ordinal scale configs
    55→
    56→    # Output
    57→    status: Literal["processing", "completed", "failed"]
    58→    error: str | None
    59→    errors: list[str]  # Accumulate partial failures per user decision
    60→

The `protocol_id` tag is already being set. What's missing is a **`run_id`** to distinguish traces from the same protocol across re-extractions, and a way to see the sequence. I'll use a ContextVar so all 7 nodes automatically pick it up without changing their signatures.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/tracing.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"MLflow tracing helpers for pipeline nodes.\n\nProvides a safe context manager that creates MLflow traces when available\nand falls back to a no-op when MLflow is not configured or installed.\n\nEach node creates its own separate top-level trace (not a child span),\ntagged with protocol_id and run_id so traces from the same pipeline run\ncan be grouped and filtered in the MLflow UI.\n\nUses ``mlflow.start_span_no_context()`` to create spans that are *not*\nattached to the global tracing context.  This guarantees each node gets\nan independent root trace regardless of whether LangChain autolog or any\nother tracing middleware has set an ambient parent trace.  The trace is\nflushed to MLflow as soon as ``span.end()`` is called (on context manager\nexit), so traces appear in real-time as nodes complete.\n\nGrouping tags set on every trace:\n    protocol_id  – groups all runs for a given protocol\n    run_id       – groups all node traces from the same pipeline invocation\n    node         – the node name (for easy filtering)\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport contextvars\nimport logging\nimport os\nfrom contextlib import contextmanager\nfrom typing import Any\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n# ContextVar set once per pipeline invocation in trigger._run_pipeline().\n# Every node's pipeline_span() reads it to tag traces with a shared run_id.\n_pipeline_run_id: contextvars.ContextVar[str] = contextvars.ContextVar(\n    \"pipeline_run_id\", default=\"\"\n)\n\n\ndef set_pipeline_run_id(run_id: str) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Set the current pipeline run_id (call before graph.ainvoke).\"\"\"\n    _pipeline_run_id.set(run_id)\n\n\n@contextmanager\ndef pipeline_span(\n    name: str,\n    span_type: str = \"CHAIN\",\n    protocol_id: str = \"\",\n):\n    \"\"\"Create a separate MLflow trace for a pipeline node.\n\n    Each call creates its own top-level trace tagged with protocol_id\n    and run_id, so individual node traces appear in MLflow as they\n    complete rather than waiting for the entire pipeline to finish.\n\n    Filter in MLflow UI:\n        tags.protocol_id = \"<id>\"       – all runs for a protocol\n        tags.run_id      = \"<run_id>\"   – single pipeline invocation\n\n    Uses ``start_span_no_context`` so the trace is independent of any\n    ambient trace context (e.g. LangChain autolog).\n\n    Args:\n        name: Trace/span name (e.g., \"ingest_node\", \"ground_node\").\n        span_type: MLflow span type (default \"CHAIN\").\n        protocol_id: Protocol ID to tag the trace with for session grouping.\n\n    Yields:\n        MLflow LiveSpan object or a no-op wrapper.\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        import mlflow\n\n        if os.getenv(\"MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI\"):\n            tags: dict[str, str] = {\"node\": name}\n            if protocol_id:\n                tags[\"protocol_id\"] = protocol_id\n            run_id = _pipeline_run_id.get()\n            if run_id:\n                tags[\"run_id\"] = run_id\n\n            span = mlflow.start_span_no_context(\n                name=name,\n                span_type=span_type,\n                tags=tags,\n            )\n            try:\n                yield span\n            except Exception:\n                span.end(status=\"ERROR\")\n                raise\n            else:\n                span.end()\n            return\n    except ImportError:\n        logger.debug(\"mlflow not installed, skipping tracing\")\n    except Exception:\n        logger.debug(\"MLflow span creation failed, falling back to no-op\")\n\n    # Fallback: no-op span\n    yield _NoOpSpan()\n\n\nclass _NoOpSpan:\n    \"\"\"No-op span that silently accepts MLflow span API calls.\"\"\"\n\n    def set_inputs(self, inputs: dict[str, Any]) -> None:\n        pass\n\n    def set_outputs(self, outputs: dict[str, Any]) -> None:\n        pass\n\n    def set_status(self, status: str) -> None:\n        pass\n"
}

   206→async def _run_pipeline(
   207→    initial_state: dict[str, Any],
   208→    config: dict[str, Any],
   209→    payload: dict[str, Any],
   210→) -> dict[str, Any]:
   211→    """Run the pipeline graph with MLflow tracing inside the async context.
   212→
   213→    MLflow ContextVars must be created in the same async context as the
   214→    LangGraph invocation. asyncio.run() creates an isolated context, so
   215→    we initialize MLflow tracing HERE (inside asyncio.run) rather than
   216→    in the sync caller.
   217→
   218→    Each pipeline node creates its own separate MLflow trace tagged with
   219→    protocol_id, so traces appear in the MLflow UI as they complete
   220→    rather than waiting for the entire pipeline. Filter by the
   221→    protocol_id tag to see all traces from a single pipeline run.
   222→    """
   223→    from protocol_processor.graph import get_graph
   224→
   225→    graph = await get_graph()
   226→
   227→    # MLflow tracing: do NOT enable langchain.autolog() here.
   228→    # Autolog wraps the entire ainvoke() in a single trace that only
   229→    # appears in MLflow after the full pipeline completes.  Instead,
   230→    # each node creates its own independent trace via pipeline_span()
   231→    # so traces appear in real-time as nodes finish.
   232→    _ensure_mlflow()
   233→
   234→    return await graph.ainvoke(initial_state, config)
   235→

Filter by the\n    run_id tag to see all traces from a single pipeline invocation.\n    \"\"\"\n    from protocol_processor.graph import get_graph\n    from protocol_processor.tracing import set_pipeline_run_id\n\n    graph = await get_graph()\n\n    # MLflow tracing: do NOT enable langchain.autolog() here.\n    # Autolog wraps the entire ainvoke() in a single trace that only\n    # appears in MLflow after the full pipeline completes. tempfile\n\nWORKER_SCRIPT = r'''\n\"\"\"Worker: runs a tiny LangGraph and reports trace details.\"\"\"\nimport asyncio, contextvars, json, os, sys, time\nfrom contextlib import contextmanager\nfrom typing import Any, TypedDict\nfrom langgraph.graph import END, START, StateGraph\n\nscenario = sys.argv[1]\ntracking_uri = sys.argv[2]\nos.environ[\"MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI\"] = tracking_uri\n\nimport mlflow\nmlflow.set_tracking_uri(tracking_uri)\nmlflow.set_experiment(f\"test-{scenario}\")\n\n# ---- ContextVar for run_id (mirrors production tracing.py) ----\n_run_id_var: contextvars.ContextVar[str] = contextvars.ContextVar(\"run_id\", default=\"\")\n\n@contextmanager\ndef pipeline_span(name, protocol_id=\"\"):\n    tags: dict[str, str] = {\"node\": name}\n    if protocol_id:\n        tags[\"protocol_id\"] = protocol_id\n    rid = _run_id_var.get()\n    if rid:\n        tags[\"run_id\"] = rid\n    s = mlflow.start_span_no_context(name=name, span_type=\"CHAIN\", tags=tags)\n    try:\n        yield s\n    except Exception:\n        s.end(status=\"ERROR\")\n        raise\n    else:\n        s.end()\n\n# ---- Tiny 3-node graph ----\nclass S(TypedDict):\n    value: int\n\nasync def node_a(state: S) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    with pipeline_span(\"node_a\", protocol_id=\"proto-abc\") as sp:\n        sp.set_inputs({\"v\": state[\"value\"]})\n        r = state[\"value\"] + 1\n        sp.set_outputs({\"r\": r})\n    return {\"value\": r}\n\nasync def node_b(state: S) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    with pipeline_span(\"node_b\", protocol_id=\"proto-abc\") as sp:\n        sp.set_inputs({\"v\": state[\"value\"]})\n        r = state[\"value\"] * 2\n        sp.set_outputs({\"r\": r})\n    return {\"value\": r}\n\nasync def node_c(state: S) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    with pipeline_span(\"node_c\", protocol_id=\"proto-abc\") as sp:\n        sp.set_inputs({\"v\": state[\"value\"]})\n        r = state[\"value\"] + 10\n        sp.set_outputs({\"r\": r})\n    return {\"value\": r}\n\ndef build():\n    g = StateGraph(S)\n    g.add_node(\"a\", node_a)\n    g.add_node(\"b\", node_b)\n    g.add_node(\"c\", node_c)\n    g.add_edge(START, \"a\")\n    g.add_edge(\"a\", \"b\")\n    g.add_edge(\"b\", \"c\")\n    g.add_edge(\"c\", END)\n    return g.compile()\n\nasync def run():\n    if scenario.startswith(\"autolog\"):\n        mlflow.langchain.autolog(run_tracer_inline=True)\n\n    # Set run_id (mirrors trigger._run_pipeline)\n    _run_id_var.set(\"proto-abc:run-001\")\n\n    graph = build()\n    await graph.ainvoke({\"value\": 1})\n    time.sleep(0.5)\n\n    client = mlflow.MlflowClient()\n    exp = client.get_experiment_by_name(f\"test-{scenario}\")\n    traces = client.search_traces(experiment_ids=[exp.experiment_id])\n\n    result = []\n    for t in traces:\n        root = t.data.spans[0] if t.data.spans else None\n        result.append({\n            \"name\": root.name if root else \"?\",\n            \"tags\": dict(t.info.tags) if t.info.tags else {},\n        })\n    result.sort(key=lambda x: x[\"name\"])\n    print(json.dumps(result))\n\nasyncio.run(run())\n'''\n\n\ndef run_scenario(scenario: str, tracking_uri: str) -> list[dict]:\n    result = subprocess.run(\n        [sys.executable, \"-c\", WORKER_SCRIPT, scenario, tracking_uri],\n        capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=30,\n    )\n    for line in result.stdout.strip().split(\"\\n\"):\n        if line.strip().startswith(\"[\"):\n            return json.loads(line.strip())\n    return [{\"error\": result.stderr[-300:] if result.stderr else \"no output\"}]\n\n\ndef main():\n    tmpdir = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=\"mlflow_trace_test_\")\n    tracking_uri = f\"file://{tmpdir}\"\n    print(f\"MLflow tracking: {tracking_uri}\\n\")\n\n    # ---- Test 1: Bug (autolog ON) ----\n    print(\"TEST 1: autolog ON (old behavior — the bug)\")\n    traces_bug = run_scenario(\"autolog-on\", tracking_uri)\n    print(f\"  Traces: {len(traces_bug)}\")\n    for t in traces_bug:\n        print(f\"    {t['name']:20s}  tags={t.get('tags', {})}\")\n    print()\n\n    # ---- Test 2: Fix (autolog OFF) ----\n    print(\"TEST 2: autolog OFF + start_span_no_context (the fix)\")\n    traces_fix = run_scenario(\"noautolog\", tracking_uri)\n    print(f\"  Traces: {len(traces_fix)}\")\n    for t in traces_fix:\n        print(f\"    {t['name']:20s}  tags={t.get('tags', {})}\")\n    print()\n\n    # ---- Verify ----\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n    bug_ok = len(traces_bug) == 1\n    fix_ok = len(traces_fix) == 3\n\n    fix_names = sorted(t[\"name\"] for t in traces_fix)\n    names_ok = fix_names == [\"node_a\", \"node_b\", \"node_c\"]\n\n    # Check all fix traces share the same run_id and protocol_id\n    fix_tags = [t.get(\"tags\", {}) for t in traces_fix]\n    run_ids = {t.get(\"run_id\", \"\") for t in fix_tags}\n    proto_ids = {t.get(\"protocol_id\", \"\") for t in fix_tags}\n    nodes = sorted(t.get(\"node\", \"\") for t in fix_tags)\n\n    tags_ok = (\n        run_ids == {\"proto-abc:run-001\"}\n        and proto_ids == {\"proto-abc\"}\n        and nodes == [\"node_a\", \"node_b\", \"node_c\"]\n    )\n\n    if bug_ok and fix_ok and names_ok and tags_ok:\n        print(\"SUCCESS!\")\n        print(f\"  Bug: 1 merged trace ('{traces_bug[0]['name']}')\")\n        print(f\"  Fix: 3 independent traces: {fix_names}\")\n        print(f\"  Grouping tags on every trace:\")\n        print(f\"    protocol_id = {proto_ids.pop()}\")\n        print(f\"    run_id      = {run_ids.pop()}\")\n        print(f\"    node        = {nodes}\")\n        print()\n        print(\"  Filter in MLflow UI:\")\n        print('    tags.run_id = \"proto-abc:run-001\"  (single pipeline run)')\n        print('    tags.protocol_id = \"proto-abc\"     (all runs for protocol)')\n    elif fix_ok and names_ok:\n        print(f\"PARTIAL: 3 traces created but tag check failed\")\n        print(f\"  run_ids={run_ids} proto_ids={proto_ids} nodes={nodes}\")\n    else:\n        print(f\"UNEXPECTED: bug={len(traces_bug)} fix={len(traces_fix)}\")\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n\n    shutil.rmtree(tmpdir, ignore_errors=True)\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n"

> AGENT

All 12 tests pass. Here's the summary of grouping tags:

**Every node trace now carries three tags:**

| Tag | Purpose | Example | MLflow UI filter |
|-----|---------|---------|-----------------|
| `protocol_id` | All runs for a protocol | `proto-abc` | `tags.protocol_id = "proto-abc"` |
| `run_id` | Single pipeline invocation | `proto-abc:run-001` | `tags.run_id = "proto-abc:run-001"` |
| `node` | Which pipeline node | `extract_node` | `tags.node = "extract_node"` |

The `run_id` uses the existing `thread_id` (`{protocol_id}:{uuid4}`) that was already being generated in `trigger.py`, so re-extractions of the same protocol get distinct `run_id` values while sharing the same `protocol_id`. The `run_id` is propagated via a `ContextVar` set once in `_run_pipeline()` before `ainvoke()`, so all 7 nodes pick it up automatically without any changes to their signatures.

> AGENT

The `start_span_no_context` is failing silently — the `except Exception` in `pipeline_span()` catches the error at DEBUG level so you never see it. The OTEL tracer provider isn't initialized because autolog used to do that, and we removed it. Let me switch to the `MlflowClient` API which writes directly to the tracking backend without depending on OTEL state.

> TOOL

     1→"""MLflow tracing helpers for pipeline nodes.
     2→
     3→Provides a safe context manager that creates MLflow traces when available
     4→and falls back to a no-op when MLflow is not configured or installed.
     5→
     6→Each node creates its own separate top-level trace (not a child span),
     7→tagged with protocol_id and run_id so traces from the same pipeline run
     8→can be grouped and filtered in the MLflow UI.
     9→
    10→Uses ``mlflow.start_span_no_context()`` to create spans that are *not*
    11→attached to the global tracing context.  This guarantees each node gets
    12→an independent root trace regardless of whether LangChain autolog or any
    13→other tracing middleware has set an ambient parent trace.  The trace is
    14→flushed to MLflow as soon as ``span.end()`` is called (on context manager
    15→exit), so traces appear in real-time as nodes complete.
    16→
    17→Grouping tags set on every trace:
    18→    protocol_id  – groups all runs for a given protocol
    19→    run_id       – groups all node traces from the same pipeline invocation
    20→    node         – the node name (for easy filtering)
    21→"""
    22→
    23→from __future__ import annotations
    24→
    25→import contextvars
    26→import logging
    27→import os
    28→from contextlib import contextmanager
    29→from typing import Any
    30→
    31→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

    33→# ContextVar set once per pipeline invocation in trigger._run_pipeline().
    34→# Every node's pipeline_span() reads it to tag traces with a shared run_id.
    35→_pipeline_run_id: contextvars.ContextVar[str] = contextvars.ContextVar(
    36→    "pipeline_run_id", default=""

    38→
    39→

    41→    """Set the current pipeline run_id (call before graph.ainvoke)."""
    42→    _pipeline_run_id.set(run_id)
    43→
    44→
    45→@contextmanager
    46→def pipeline_span(
    47→    name: str,
    48→    span_type: str = "CHAIN",
    49→    protocol_id: str = "",
    50→):
    51→    """Create a separate MLflow trace for a pipeline node.
    52→
    53→    Each call creates its own top-level trace tagged with protocol_id
    54→    and run_id, so individual node traces appear in MLflow as they
    55→    complete rather than waiting for the entire pipeline to finish.
    56→
    57→    Filter in MLflow UI:
    58→        tags.protocol_id = "<id>"       – all runs for a protocol

    62→    ambient trace context (e.g. LangChain autolog).
    63→
    64→    Args:

    66→        span_type: MLflow span type (default "CHAIN").

> AGENT

Good catch — the `@mlflow.trace` / `mlflow.start_span()` fluent API is the right approach. The `start_span_no_context()` was failing silently because the OTEL tracer provider wasn't initialized (autolog used to handle that). The standard fluent API handles initialization lazily.

Let me rewrite `tracing.py` to use the standard fluent API — `mlflow.start_span()` for the context manager and `mlflow.update_current_trace()` for tags, exactly matching MLflow's documented pattern.

> TOOL

{
  "file_path": "/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/tracing.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"MLflow tracing helpers for pipeline nodes.\n\nProvides a safe context manager that creates MLflow traces when available\nand falls back to a no-op when MLflow is not configured or installed.\n\nEach node creates its own separate top-level trace (not a child span),\ntagged with protocol_id and run_id so traces from the same pipeline run\ncan be grouped and filtered in the MLflow UI.\n\nIMPORTANT: ``mlflow.langchain.autolog()`` must NOT be enabled.  Autolog\nwraps ``graph.ainvoke()`` in a single parent trace and all node spans\nbecome children — producing one constantly-updating trace that only\nappears after the full pipeline finishes.  Without autolog, each call to\n``mlflow.start_span()`` inside a node creates an independent root trace\nthat is flushed to MLflow as soon as the context manager exits.\n\nGrouping tags set on every trace:\n    protocol_id  – groups all runs for a given protocol\n    run_id       – groups all node traces from the same pipeline invocation\n    node         – the node name (for easy filtering)\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport contextvars\nimport logging\nimport os\nfrom contextlib import contextmanager\nfrom typing import Any\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n# ContextVar set once per pipeline invocation in trigger._run_pipeline().\n# Every node's pipeline_span() reads it to tag traces with a shared run_id.\n_pipeline_run_id: contextvars.ContextVar[str] = contextvars.ContextVar(\n    \"pipeline_run_id\", default=\"\"\n)\n\n\ndef set_pipeline_run_id(run_id: str) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Set the current pipeline run_id (call before graph.ainvoke).\"\"\"\n    _pipeline_run_id.set(run_id)\n\n\n@contextmanager\ndef pipeline_span(\n    name: str,\n    span_type: str = \"CHAIN\",\n    protocol_id: str = \"\",\n):\n    \"\"\"Create a separate MLflow trace for a pipeline node.\n\n    Each call creates its own top-level trace tagged with protocol_id\n    and run_id, so individual node traces appear in MLflow as they\n    complete rather than waiting for the entire pipeline to finish.\n\n    Filter in MLflow UI:\n        tags.protocol_id = \"<id>\"       – all runs for a protocol\n        tags.run_id      = \"<run_id>\"   – single pipeline invocation\n\n    Args:\n        name: Trace/span name (e.g., \"ingest_node\", \"ground_node\").\n        span_type: MLflow span type (default \"CHAIN\").\n        protocol_id: Protocol ID to tag the trace with for session grouping.\n\n    Yields:\n        MLflow Span object or a no-op wrapper.\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        import mlflow\n\n        if os.getenv(\"MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI\"):\n            with mlflow.start_span(name=name, span_type=span_type) as span:\n                # Tag the trace for grouping/filtering in the MLflow UI.\n                tags: dict[str, str] = {\"node\": name}\n                if protocol_id:\n                    tags[\"protocol_id\"] = protocol_id\n                run_id = _pipeline_run_id.get()\n                if run_id:\n                    tags[\"run_id\"] = run_id\n                try:\n                    mlflow.update_current_trace(tags=tags)\n                except Exception:\n                    logger.debug(\"MLflow tag update failed (non-fatal)\")\n                yield span\n                return\n    except ImportError:\n        logger.debug(\"mlflow not installed, skipping tracing\")\n    except Exception:\n        logger.warning(\"MLflow span creation failed, falling back to no-op\", exc_info=True)\n\n    # Fallback: no-op span\n    yield _NoOpSpan()\n\n\nclass _NoOpSpan:\n    \"\"\"No-op span that silently accepts MLflow span API calls.\"\"\"\n\n    def set_inputs(self, inputs: dict[str, Any]) -> None:\n        pass\n\n    def set_outputs(self, outputs: dict[str, Any]) -> None:\n        pass\n\n    def set_status(self, status: str) -> None:\n        pass\n"
}

tempfile\n\nWORKER_SCRIPT = r'''\n\"\"\"Worker: runs a tiny LangGraph and reports trace details.\"\"\"\nimport asyncio, contextvars, json, os, sys, time\nfrom contextlib import contextmanager\nfrom typing import Any, TypedDict\nfrom langgraph.graph import END, START, StateGraph\n\nscenario = sys.argv[1]\ntracking_uri = sys.argv[2]\nos.environ[\"MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI\"] = tracking_uri\n\nimport mlflow\nmlflow.set_tracking_uri(tracking_uri)\nmlflow.set_experiment(f\"test-{scenario}\")\n\n# ---- ContextVar for run_id (mirrors production tracing.py) ----\n_run_id_var: contextvars.ContextVar[str] = contextvars.ContextVar(\"run_id\", default=\"\")\n\n@contextmanager\ndef pipeline_span(name, protocol_id=\"\"):\n    \"\"\"Mirrors production tracing.py — uses mlflow.start_span (fluent API).\"\"\"\n    with mlflow.start_span(name=name, span_type=\"CHAIN\") as span:\n        tags = {\"node\": name}\n        if protocol_id:\n            tags[\"protocol_id\"] = protocol_id\n        rid = _run_id_var.get()\n        if rid:\n            tags[\"run_id\"] = rid\n        try:\n            mlflow.update_current_trace(tags=tags)\n        except Exception:\n            pass\n        yield span\n\n# ---- Tiny 3-node graph ----\nclass S(TypedDict):\n    value: int\n\nasync def node_a(state: S) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    with pipeline_span(\"node_a\", protocol_id=\"proto-abc\") as sp:\n        sp.set_inputs({\"v\": state[\"value\"]})\n        r = state[\"value\"] + 1\n        sp.set_outputs({\"r\": r})\n    return {\"value\": r}\n\nasync def node_b(state: S) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    with pipeline_span(\"node_b\", protocol_id=\"proto-abc\") as sp:\n        sp.set_inputs({\"v\": state[\"value\"]})\n        r = state[\"value\"] * 2\n        sp.set_outputs({\"r\": r})\n    return {\"value\": r}\n\nasync def node_c(state: S) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    with pipeline_span(\"node_c\", protocol_id=\"proto-abc\") as sp:\n        sp.set_inputs({\"v\": state[\"value\"]})\n        r = state[\"value\"] + 10\n        sp.set_outputs({\"r\": r})\n    return {\"value\": r}\n\ndef build():\n    g = StateGraph(S)\n    g.add_node(\"a\", node_a)\n    g.add_node(\"b\", node_b)\n    g.add_node(\"c\", node_c)\n    g.add_edge(START, \"a\")\n    g.add_edge(\"a\", \"b\")\n    g.add_edge(\"b\", \"c\")\n    g.add_edge(\"c\", END)\n    return g.compile()\n\nasync def run():\n    if scenario.startswith(\"autolog\"):\n        mlflow.langchain.autolog(run_tracer_inline=True)\n\n    # Set run_id (mirrors trigger._run_pipeline)\n    _run_id_var.set(\"proto-abc:run-001\")\n\n    graph = build()\n    await graph.ainvoke({\"value\": 1})\n    time.sleep(0.5)\n\n    client = mlflow.MlflowClient()\n    exp = client.get_experiment_by_name(f\"test-{scenario}\")\n    traces = client.search_traces(experiment_ids=[exp.experiment_id])\n\n    result = []\n    for t in traces:\n        root = t.data.spans[0] if t.data.spans else None\n        result.append({\n            \"name\": root.name if root else \"?\",\n            \"tags\": {k: v for k, v in (t.info.tags or {}).items()\n                     if not k.startswith(\"mlflow.\")},\n        })\n    result.sort(key=lambda x: x[\"name\"])\n    print(json.dumps(result))\n\nasyncio.run(run())\n'''\n\n\ndef run_scenario(scenario: str, tracking_uri: str) -> list[dict]:\n    result = subprocess.run(\n        [sys.executable, \"-c\", WORKER_SCRIPT, scenario, tracking_uri],\n        capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=30,\n    )\n    for line in result.stdout.strip().split(\"\\n\"):\n        if line.strip().startswith(\"[\"):\n            return json.loads(line.strip())\n    return [{\"error\": result.stderr[-500:] if result.stderr else \"no output\"}]\n\n\ndef main():\n    tmpdir = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=\"mlflow_trace_test_\")\n    tracking_uri = f\"file://{tmpdir}\"\n    print(f\"MLflow tracking: {tracking_uri}\\n\")\n\n    # ---- Test 1: Bug (autolog ON) ----\n    print(\"TEST 1: autolog ON (old behavior — the bug)\")\n    traces_bug = run_scenario(\"autolog-on\", tracking_uri)\n    print(f\"  Traces: {len(traces_bug)}\")\n    for t in traces_bug:\n        print(f\"    {t['name']:20s}  tags={t.get('tags', {})}\")\n    print()\n\n    # ---- Test 2: Fix (autolog OFF, standard fluent API) ----\n    print(\"TEST 2: autolog OFF + mlflow.start_span (the fix)\")\n    traces_fix = run_scenario(\"noautolog\", tracking_uri)\n    print(f\"  Traces: {len(traces_fix)}\")\n    for t in traces_fix:\n        print(f\"    {t['name']:20s}  tags={t.get('tags', {})}\")\n    print()\n\n    # ---- Verify ----\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n    fix_names = sorted(t[\"name\"] for t in traces_fix)\n    fix_tags = [t.get(\"tags\", {}) for t in traces_fix]\n    run_ids = {t.get(\"run_id\", \"\") for t in fix_tags}\n    proto_ids = {t.get(\"protocol_id\", \"\") for t in fix_tags}\n    nodes = sorted(t.get(\"node\", \"\") for t in fix_tags)\n\n    ok = (\n        len(traces_bug) <= 1\n        and len(traces_fix) == 3\n        and fix_names == [\"node_a\", \"node_b\", \"node_c\"]\n        and run_ids == {\"proto-abc:run-001\"}\n        and proto_ids == {\"proto-abc\"}\n        and nodes == [\"node_a\", \"node_b\", \"node_c\"]\n    )\n\n    if ok:\n        print(\"SUCCESS!\")\n        print(f\"  Bug: {len(traces_bug)} merged trace(s)\")\n        print(f\"  Fix: 3 independent traces: {fix_names}\")\n        print(f\"  Grouping tags on every trace:\")\n        print(f\"    protocol_id = {proto_ids.pop()}\")\n        print(f\"    run_id      = {run_ids.pop()}\")\n        print(f\"    node        = {nodes}\")\n        print()\n        print(\"  Filter in MLflow UI:\")\n        print('    tags.run_id = \"<run_id>\"          -> single pipeline run')\n        print('    tags.protocol_id = \"<proto_id>\"   -> all runs for protocol')\n    else:\n        print(f\"UNEXPECTED: bug={len(traces_bug)}, fix={len(traces_fix)}\")\n        print(f\"  fix_names={fix_names}\")\n        print(f\"  run_ids={run_ids} proto_ids={proto_ids} nodes={nodes}\")\n        for t in traces_fix:\n            if \"error\" in t:\n                print(f\"  error: {t['error']}\")\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n\n    shutil.rmtree(tmpdir, ignore_errors=True)\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n"

mlflow.update_current_trace(tags=tags)\n        except Exception:\n            pass\n        yield span\n\n\n# ---- Tiny 3-node graph ----\nclass S(TypedDict):\n    value: int\n\n\nasync def state[\"value\"] + 1})\n    return {\"value\": state[\"value\"] + 1}\n\n\nasync def node_b(state: S) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    with pipeline_span(\"node_b\", protocol_id=\"proto-X\") as sp:\n        sp.set_inputs({\"v\": state[\"value\"]})\n        sp.set_outputs({\"r\": state[\"value\"] * 2})\n    return {\"value\": state[\"value\"] * 2}\n\n\nasync def node_c(state: S) -> dict[str, Any]:\n g.add_edge(\"a\", \"b\")\n    g.add_edge(\"b\", \"c\")\n    g.add_edge(\"c\", END)\n    return g.compile()\n\n\n# ---- Production execution pattern ----\n\n\nasync def _run_pipeline(graph: Any) ->   # Mirror outbox: copy context, run in thread executor\n    executor = ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=1, thread_name_prefix=\"outbox\")\n    ctx = contextvars.copy_context()\n    future = executor.submit(ctx.run, sync_handler, graph)\n    future.result(timeout=30)\n    executor.shutdown(wait=True)\n\n    time.sleep(0.5)\n\n    # Check results\n    client = mlflow.MlflowClient()\n    exp = client.get_experiment_by_name(\"prod-test\")\n    traces = client.search_traces(experiment_ids=[exp.experiment_id])\n\n    names = sorted([t.data.spans[0].name for t in traces if t.data.spans])\n    print(f\"Traces found: {len(traces)}\")\n    for t in traces:\n        root = t.data.spans[0] if t.data.spans else None\n        user_tags = {k: v for k, v in (t.info.tags or {}).items()\n                     if not k.startswith(\"mlflow.\")}\n        print(f\"  {root.name:20s}  tags={user_tags}\")\n\n    print()\n    if len(traces) == 3 and names == [\"node_a\", > AGENT

All good. Here's what changed from the previous attempt:

**The issue**: `start_span_no_context()` bypasses MLflow's lazy OTEL tracer provider initialization, so it silently failed (caught by `except Exception` at DEBUG level — invisible in your logs).

**The fix**: Switched back to the standard fluent API `mlflow.start_span()` + `mlflow.update_current_trace(tags=...)`, which handles OTEL initialization lazily on first use. Without autolog creating a parent trace, each `start_span()` call in a node creates an independent root trace. Also upgraded the error log from `debug` to `warning` so any future failures are visible.

The error was being silently swallowed at line 99 of the old `tracing.py`:
```python
except Exception:
    logger.debug("MLflow span creation failed, falling back to no-op")  # invisible!
```

     1→"""MLflow tracing helpers for pipeline nodes.
     2→
     3→Provides a safe context manager that creates MLflow traces when available
     4→and falls back to a no-op when MLflow is not configured or installed.
     5→
     6→Each node creates its own separate top-level trace (not a child span),
     7→tagged with protocol_id and run_id so traces from the same pipeline run
     8→can be grouped and filtered in the MLflow UI.
     9→
    10→IMPORTANT: ``mlflow.langchain.autolog()`` must NOT be enabled.  Autolog
    11→wraps ``graph.ainvoke()`` in a single parent trace and all node spans
    12→become children — producing one constantly-updating trace that only
    13→appears after the full pipeline finishes.  Without autolog, each call to
    14→``mlflow.start_span()`` inside a node creates an independent root trace
    15→that is flushed to MLflow as soon as the context manager exits.
    16→
    17→Grouping tags set on every trace:
    18→    protocol_id  – groups all runs for a given protocol
    19→    run_id       – groups all node traces from the same pipeline invocation
    20→    node         – the node name (for easy filtering)
    21→"""
    22→
    23→from __future__ import annotations
    24→
    25→import contextvars
    26→import logging
    27→import os
    28→from contextlib import contextmanager
    29→from typing import Any
    30→
    31→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    32→
    33→# ContextVar set once per pipeline invocation in trigger._run_pipeline().
    34→# Every node's pipeline_span() reads it to tag traces with a shared run_id.
    35→_pipeline_run_id: contextvars.ContextVar[str] = contextvars.ContextVar(
    36→    "pipeline_run_id", default=""
    37→)
    38→
    39→
    40→def set_pipeline_run_id(run_id: str) -> None:
    41→    """Set the current pipeline run_id (call before graph.ainvoke)."""
    42→    _pipeline_run_id.set(run_id)
    43→
    44→
    45→@contextmanager
    46→def pipeline_span(
    47→    name: str,
    48→    span_type: str = "CHAIN",
    49→    protocol_id: str = "",
    50→):
    51→    """Create a separate MLflow trace for a pipeline node.
    52→
    53→    Each call creates its own top-level trace tagged with protocol_id
    54→    and run_id, so individual node traces appear in MLflow as they
    55→    complete rather than waiting for the entire pipeline to finish.
    56→
    57→    Filter in MLflow UI:
    58→        tags.protocol_id = "<id>"       – all runs for a protocol
    59→        tags.run_id      = "<run_id>"   – single pipeline invocation
    60→
    61→    Args:
    62→        name: Trace/span name (e.g., "ingest_node", "ground_node").
    63→        span_type: MLflow span type (default "CHAIN").
    64→        protocol_id: Protocol ID to tag the trace with for session grouping.
    65→
    66→    Yields:
    67→        MLflow Span object or a no-op wrapper.
    68→    """
    69→    try:
    70→        import mlflow
    71→
    72→        if os.getenv("MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI"):
    73→            with mlflow.start_span(name=name, span_type=span_type) as span:
    74→                # Tag the trace for grouping/filtering in the MLflow UI.
    75→                tags: dict[str, str] = {"node": name}
    76→                if protocol_id:
    77→                    tags["protocol_id"] = protocol_id
    78→                run_id = _pipeline_run_id.get()
    79→                if run_id:
    80→                    tags["run_id"] = run_id
    81→                try:
    82→                    mlflow.update_current_trace(tags=tags)
    83→                except Exception:
    84→                    logger.debug("MLflow tag update failed (non-fatal)")
    85→                yield span
    86→                return
    87→    except ImportError:
    88→        logger.debug("mlflow not installed, skipping tracing")
    89→    except Exception:
    90→        logger.warning("MLflow span creation failed, falling back to no-op", exc_info=True)
    91→
    92→    # Fallback: no-op span
    93→    yield _NoOpSpan()
    94→
    95→
    96→class _NoOpSpan:
    97→    """No-op span that silently accepts MLflow span API calls."""
    98→
    99→    def set_inputs(self, inputs: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   100→        pass
   101→
   102→    def set_outputs(self, outputs: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   103→        pass
   104→
   105→    def set_status(self, status: str) -> None:
   106→        pass
   107→
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/scripts/verify_mlflow_tracing.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Verify MLflow per-node tracing: independent traces, grouping tags,\nand real-time appearance (traces show up as nodes complete, not batched).\n\nRun:  uv run python scripts/verify_mlflow_tracing.py\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport asyncio\nimport contextvars\nimport json\nimport os\nimport shutil\nimport tempfile\nimport time\nfrom concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor\nfrom contextlib import contextmanager\nfrom typing import Any, TypedDict\n\nfrom langgraph.graph import END, START, StateGraph\n\n# ---- Setup ----\ntmpdir = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=\"mlflow_trace_test_\")\ntracking_uri = f\"file://{tmpdir}\"\nos.environ[\"MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI\"] = tracking_uri\n\nimport mlflow\n\nmlflow.set_tracking_uri(tracking_uri)\nmlflow.set_experiment(\"trace-test\")\n\n# ---- tracing.py mirror ----\n_run_id_var: contextvars.ContextVar[str] = contextvars.ContextVar(\n    \"pipeline_run_id\", default=\"\"\n)\n\n\n@contextmanager\ndef pipeline_span(name: str, protocol_id: str = \"\"):\n    with mlflow.start_span(name=name, span_type=\"CHAIN\") as span:\n        tags: dict[str, str] = {\"node\": name}\n        if protocol_id:\n            tags[\"protocol_id\"] = protocol_id\n        rid = _run_id_var.get()\n        if rid:\n            tags[\"run_id\"] = rid\n        try:\n            mlflow.update_current_trace(tags=tags)\n        except Exception:\n            pass\n        yield span\n\n\ndef count_traces() -> int:\n    client = mlflow.MlflowClient()\n    exp = client.get_experiment_by_name(\"trace-test\")\n    if not exp:\n        return 0\n    return len(client.search_traces(experiment_ids=[exp.experiment_id]))\n\n\ndef get_traces() -> list[dict]:\n    client = mlflow.MlflowClient()\n    exp = client.get_experiment_by_name(\"trace-test\")\n    if not exp:\n        return []\n    traces = client.search_traces(experiment_ids=[exp.experiment_id])\n    out = []\n    for t in traces:\n        root = t.data.spans[0] if t.data.spans else None\n        out.append({\n            \"name\": root.name if root else \"?\",\n            \"tags\": {k: v for k, v in (t.info.tags or {}).items()\n                     if not k.startswith(\"mlflow.\")},\n        })\n    return sorted(out, key=lambda x: x[\"name\"])\n\n\n# ---- Graph with delay to observe real-time behavior ----\nclass S(TypedDict):\n    value: int\n    trace_counts: list[int]  # snapshot trace count after each node\n\n\nasync def node_a(state: S) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    with pipeline_span(\"node_a\", protocol_id=\"proto-X\") as sp:\n        sp.set_inputs({\"v\": state[\"value\"]})\n        await asyncio.sleep(0.1)\n        sp.set_outputs({\"r\": state[\"value\"] + 1})\n    # After span closes, trace should be flushed — check count\n    n = count_traces()\n    return {\"value\": state[\"value\"] + 1, \"trace_counts\": [n]}\n\n\nasync def node_b(state: S) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    with pipeline_span(\"node_b\", protocol_id=\"proto-X\") as sp:\n        sp.set_inputs({\"v\": state[\"value\"]})\n        await asyncio.sleep(0.1)\n        sp.set_outputs({\"r\": state[\"value\"] * 2})\n    n = count_traces()\n    return {\"value\": state[\"value\"] * 2, \"trace_counts\": [n]}\n\n\nasync def node_c(state: S) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    with pipeline_span(\"node_c\", protocol_id=\"proto-X\") as sp:\n        sp.set_inputs({\"v\": state[\"value\"]})\n        await asyncio.sleep(0.1)\n        sp.set_outputs({\"r\": state[\"value\"] + 10})\n    n = count_traces()\n    return {\"value\": state[\"value\"] + 10, \"trace_counts\": [n]}\n\n\ndef build():\n    g = StateGraph(S)\n    g.add_node(\"a\", node_a)\n    g.add_node(\"b\", node_b)\n    g.add_node(\"c\", node_c)\n    g.add_edge(START, \"a\")\n    g.add_edge(\"a\", \"b\")\n    g.add_edge(\"b\", \"c\")\n    g.add_edge(\"c\", END)\n    return g.compile()\n\n\nasync def _run(graph: Any) -> dict:\n    mlflow.set_tracking_uri(os.environ[\"MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI\"])\n    mlflow.set_experiment(\"trace-test\")\n    _run_id_var.set(\"proto-X:run-42\")\n    return await graph.ainvoke({\"value\": 1, \"trace_counts\": []})\n\n\ndef sync_handler(graph: Any) -> dict:\n    return asyncio.run(_run(graph))\n\n\ndef main():\n    print(f\"MLflow tracking: {tracking_uri}\\n\")\n\n    graph = build()\n\n    # Run in thread executor (mirrors production outbox pattern)\n    executor = ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=1, thread_name_prefix=\"outbox\")\n    ctx = contextvars.copy_context()\n    future = executor.submit(ctx.run, sync_handler, graph)\n    result = future.result(timeout=30)\n    executor.shutdown(wait=True)\n\n    trace_counts = result[\"trace_counts\"]\n    traces = get_traces()\n\n    # ---- Real-time check ----\n    print(\"1. REAL-TIME TRACE APPEARANCE\")\n    print(f\"   Traces visible after node_a: {trace_counts[0]}\")\n    print(f\"   Traces visible after node_b: {trace_counts[1]}\")\n    print(f\"   Traces visible after node_c: {trace_counts[2]}\")\n    realtime_ok = (\n        trace_counts[0] >= 1\n        and trace_counts[1] >= 2\n        and trace_counts[2] >= 3\n    )\n    print(f\"   Incremental: {'YES' if realtime_ok else 'NO'}\")\n    print()\n\n    # ---- Independent traces check ----\n    print(\"2. INDEPENDENT TRACES\")\n    print(f\"   Total: {len(traces)}\")\n    for t in traces:\n        print(f\"     {t['name']:15s}  {t['tags']}\")\n    independent_ok = (\n        len(traces) == 3\n        and sorted(t[\"name\"] for t in traces) == [\"node_a\", \"node_b\", \"node_c\"]\n    )\n    print(f\"   3 separate traces: {'YES' if independent_ok else 'NO'}\")\n    print()\n\n    # ---- Grouping tags check ----\n    print(\"3. GROUPING TAGS\")\n    tags = [t[\"tags\"] for t in traces]\n    run_ids = {t.get(\"run_id\") for t in tags}\n    proto_ids = {t.get(\"protocol_id\") for t in tags}\n    nodes = sorted(t.get(\"node\", \"\") for t in tags)\n    tags_ok = (\n        run_ids == {\"proto-X:run-42\"}\n        and proto_ids == {\"proto-X\"}\n        and nodes == [\"node_a\", \"node_b\", \"node_c\"]\n    )\n    print(f\"   Shared run_id:      {run_ids}\")\n    print(f\"   Shared protocol_id: {proto_ids}\")\n    print(f\"   Distinct node tags: {nodes}\")\n    print(f\"   Consistent: {'YES' if tags_ok else 'NO'}\")\n    print()\n\n    # ---- Verdict ----\n    print(\"=\" * 50)\n    if realtime_ok and independent_ok and tags_ok:\n        print(\"ALL CHECKS PASSED\")\n    else:\n        failures = []\n        if not realtime_ok:\n            failures.append(\"real-time\")\n        if not independent_ok:\n            failures.append(\"independence\")\n        if not tags_ok:\n            failures.append(\"tags\")\n        print(f\"FAILED: {', '.join(failures)}\")\n    print(\"=\" * 50)\n\n    del os.environ[\"MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI\"]\n    shutil.rmtree(tmpdir, ignore_errors=True)\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n"
}

Now I see the full chain. At `structure_builder.py:186`:
```python
value_str = fm.get("value", "")
```
If `fm["value"]` is a dict (e.g. `{"amount": 18, "unit": "years"}` from the LLM), then `_parse_value` at line 66 catches `ValueError`/`TypeError` on `float(dict)` and returns `(None, the_dict)` as `value_text`. Then `normalize_value(the_dict)` blows up on `.strip()`.

The fix is to coerce `value` to a string at the boundary where it enters `_create_atomic_from_mapping`. Let me check what other fields might have the same issue.

222 pass. The fix:

**Root cause**: The LLM (Gemini) sometimes returns `value` as a dict (e.g. `{"amount": 18, "unit": "years"}`) instead of a plain string in field mappings. This dict flowed through `_parse_value` → `normalize_value` → `.strip()` crash.

**Fix** — coerce non-string values to strings at three boundaries:
1. `structure_builder.py:186` — `fm.get("value")` coerced before `_parse_value`
2. `unit_normalizer.py:normalize_unit` — guards against dict/int/list input
3. `unit_normalizer.py:normalize_value` — guards against dict/int/list input

Perfect! Now I have a complete picture. Let me create a comprehensive summary report.

## Summary: Field Mapping Data Flow and Root Cause Analysis

### Root Cause Found

The issue is that `fm.get("value")` returns a **dictionary** instead of a string because **recent changes (commit 05b6c5e) converted the field_mapping value structure from flat strings to typed objects**.

### Data Flow Trace (Backwards)

#### 1. **structure_builder.py - `_create_atomic_from_mapping` (lines 167-234)**

**Problem Location:** Line 186
```python
value_str = fm.get("value", "")
if not isinstance(value_str, str):
    value_str = str(value_str) if value_str is not None else ""
```

The fix has already been applied (lines 187-188), but this is **defensive code** added to handle the fact that `value` is now a dict.

**What it does:** The function attempts to handle both formats:
- **Old format (flat):** `value="7"` (string)
- **New format (typed):** `value={"type": "standard", "value": "7", "unit": "%"}` (dict)

When `value` is a dict, `str(value)` produces something like `"{'type': 'standard', ...}"`, which then fails in `_parse_value()`.

#### 2. **structure.py - Lines 47-48**

Field mappings are retrieved from the Criteria model's conditions dict:
```python
conditions = criterion.conditions or {}
field_mappings = conditions.get("field_mappings", [])
```

The `conditions` is stored as JSONB in the database.

#### 3. **Database Storage - Criteria.conditions (JSONB)**

Field mappings are stored in `criteria.conditions["field_mappings"]` as JSONB. The format has changed from:
```json
{
  "field_mappings": [
    {
      "entity": "HbA1c",
      "relation": "=",
      "value": "7",
      "unit": "%",
      "entity_code": "...",
      "entity_system": "..."
    }
  ]
}
```

To the new format:
```json
{
  "field_mappings": [
    {
      "entity": "HbA1c",
      "relation": "=",
      "value": {
        "type": "standard",
        "value": "7",
        "unit": "%"
      },
      "entity_code": "...",
      "entity_system": "..."
    }
  ]
}
```

#### 4. **field_mapper.py - `generate_field_mappings` (lines 101-193)**

**KEY CHANGE:** Lines 165-176 (in the file content I read)

```python
mappings = [
    {
        "entity": m.entity,
        "relation": _normalize_relation(m.relation),
        "value": m.value.model_dump(exclude_none=True),  # ← NOW A DICT!
        "entity_code": entity.selected_code,  # ← RENAMED (was entity_concept_id)
        "entity_system": entity.selected_system,  # ← RENAMED (was entity_concept_system)
        "omop_concept_id": entity.omop_concept_id,
        "entity_type": entity.entity_type,
    }
    for m in response.mappings
]
```

**The FieldMappingValue schema (lines 47-70):**

```python
class FieldMappingValue(BaseModel):
    """Typed value object with type discriminator."""
    type: Literal["standard", "range", "temporal"]
    value: str | None = None
    unit: str | None = None
    min: str | None = None  # for range type
    max: str | None = None  # for range type
    duration: str | None = None  # for temporal type
```

Line 169 calls `m.value.model_dump(exclude_none=True)`, which converts the Pydantic model to a **dictionary**.

#### 5. **Recent Changes - Commit 05b6c5e**

The commit message states:
```
fix: normalize field_mapper output to match frontend schema

- Add FieldMappingValue Pydantic model with type discriminator
  (standard/range/temporal) for typed value objects
- Add _normalize_relation() to map legacy operators
- Update Gemini prompt to instruct producing typed value objects
- Rename entity_concept_id -> entity_code
- Rename entity_concept_system -> entity_system
- Call model_dump(exclude_none=True) on value objects for clean JSON
```

### What Changed

| Aspect | Before | After |
|--------|--------|-------|
| **value type** | String: `"7"` | Dict: `{"type": "standard", "value": "7", "unit": "%"}` |
| **value field in DB** | Flat string | Typed object with discriminator |
| **entity_concept_id** | Used | Renamed to `entity_code` |
| **entity_concept_system** | Used | Renamed to `entity_system` |
| **relation operators** | Legacy (has, is, not, ==, range) | Normalized (contains, =, not_contains, within) |

### Defensive Code Added

Three files have defensive type conversions added in git diff (lines 187-188 in structure_builder.py):

1. **structure_builder.py:187-188**: Convert dict value to string
2. **unit_normalizer.py:75-76**: Convert unit to string if not already
3. **unit_normalizer.py:100-101**: Convert value_text to string if not already

This defensive code prevents crashes but produces incorrect parsing because it converts `{"type": "standard", "value": "7", "unit": "%"}` to the string representation `"{'type': 'standard', 'value': '7', 'unit': '%'}"`.

### The Real Fix Needed

Instead of converting the dict to a string in structure_builder.py, the code should **extract the actual value from the typed object**:

```python
value_obj = fm.get("value", "")
if isinstance(value_obj, dict):
    # Extract value from typed object
    value_str = value_obj.get("value", "")
else:
    # Legacy flat string format
    value_str = value_obj
```

Similar logic should apply to `unit` handling at line 194-195.

---

## File Contents Summary

I've provided you:
1. **field_mapper.py** - Full content showing the FieldMappingValue model and generate_field_mappings function
2. **structure.py** - Shows how field_mappings are retrieved from Criteria.conditions
3. **structure_builder.py** - Shows the current defensive code (lines 186-188) that converts dict to string
4. **gemini_utils.py** - Schema utility functions
5. **Git diff** - Shows all recent changes including the defensive type conversions

The core issue: **field_mapper now generates typed value objects (dicts), but structure_builder still expects flat strings and has only defensive conversion code rather than proper extraction logic.**

```
